PROMPT TB2-ironworks (22 October 2026), the textured route after the pilot TB1. Paint 4 pictures for the isometric railway game in this repository: the Ironworks (`ironworks`), views r0, r1, r2, r3, in this order. Every picture is painted OVER a TEXTURED 3D BLOCKOUT RENDER of that view; the building's ORIGINAL r0 PAINTING is the reference for the look.

WHY. The author tried two earlier ways of getting the turned views: free painting on grey guides (the camera was lost) and painting over AI-reconstructed scaffolds (morphed shapes; held and now superseded). Core then built each building as a clean Blender blockout matched to the painted r0, projected the original painting onto it, and rendered all four views at the game's exact camera with the game's sun: the render's camera, proportions and every part's position are exact. What the render is not is one coherent painting: its back walls and far roof slopes are clones and mirror images (they repeat verbatim and are a little blocky and uniform), the patches meet in seams, and the thin and round parts are smeared by the projection onto plain boxes and cylinders. TB1 tested the method on the warehouse and the water pump: all eight views were accepted (IoU 0.958 to 0.983), the pictures read as one coherent painting in the original's hand. TB1's power plant failed because the full original painting was attached: the tool copied its size, its position and its glow. Keep the same discipline: paint over the render, never copy the reference's size or position.

THE JOB, in one paragraph. Paint over the textured render. Keep its silhouette, camera, proportions and every part's position exactly. Unify its surface into ONE coherent painting in the style and materials of the building's original r0 painting: fix the cloned and smeared patches, the seams and the blocky hidden sides, and finish the thin parts crisply. The lighting follows the game sun as in the render: the lit side stays where the render has it. No new elements, nothing removed.

LANE AND FILES. Branch art/textured-b (above). Touch nothing outside assets/source/textured/ironworks/ (one folder, with raw/ inside) and your lane's measuring script assets/source/textured/measure-textured-b.mjs (the first prompt of this group creates it, later prompts of the group reuse and, if they must, extend it; each lane has its own file so that the lanes do not collide). Never push to main or develop. Commit subjects read "Art: <outcome>". Keep every picture your image tool returns, byte for byte, as assets/source/textured/ironworks/raw/ironworks-r<v>-attempt-<k>.png (v the view, k from 1 for each view; if assets/source/textured/ironworks/prompts.json already holds attempts of a view (an earlier run), go on from its highest k and never overwrite or attach one; a view that an earlier run already accepted is not painted again), and record each call in assets/source/textured/ironworks/prompts.json: {view, attempt, file, prompt, references (in order), returned (the size it came back), measured (the numbers below), kept, note}. Never delete or overwrite a picture. Never commit the renders or the originals you fetch (below). Ask the tool for the canvas size (1536 x 1024), a transparent background, and record the size it returns (a 1024 px wide canvas has come back 1254 px wide; 1536 x 1024 came back as asked).
Accepted pictures (and only accepted ones) are copied byte for byte from raw/ to assets/source/textured/ironworks/ironworks-r<v>.png.

THE INPUTS. Make two folders OUTSIDE the repository, on G: next to your worktree: ../scaffolds-textured-b and ../originals-textured-b (they are this group's own; ironworks files only). Write the files out of git byte for byte (a PowerShell redirect corrupts a PNG: use bash, or cmd /c, or git cat-file blob with --filters off). From the repository root, in bash:

  git fetch origin claude/art-scaffolds-review-2 develop
  mkdir -p ../scaffolds-textured-b ../originals-textured-b
  for v in 0 1 2 3; do for f in textured.png bare.png; do git show origin/claude/art-scaffolds-review-2:assets/source/buildings-v2/scaffolds-textured/ironworks/ironworks-r$v-$f > ../scaffolds-textured-b/ironworks-r$v-$f; done; git show origin/claude/art-scaffolds-review-2:assets/source/buildings-v2/scaffolds-textured/ironworks/ironworks-r$v.json > ../scaffolds-textured-b/ironworks-r$v.json; done
  git fetch origin develop
  git show origin/develop:assets/source/base-v1/works-full-ironworks.png > ../originals-textured-b/ironworks.png

The original is the building picture as the game has it today and it stays as it is: origin/develop:assets/source/base-v1/works-full-ironworks.png. Check its sha256 first, on the bytes you wrote: 2b709e0386c0b2d35a271dc63c46eba550884056a312f1cf0f72003d84195283. If it differs, stop and say so. Its size is 1536 x 1024; it may have a dark glow or shadow round the building: that is not part of what you paint.
Per view: ironworks-r<v>-textured.png is the render to PAINT OVER (variant B of the pilot: the painting's own shading taken out and the model relit with the game's sun, upper left): its shape, camera, proportions, layout, light and colours are right, its surface is not one painting. ironworks-r<v>-bare.png (the same shape in plain clay) and ironworks-r<v>.json (outline_polygon_px, the silhouette in canvas px; the base and footprint corners; the camera numbers; the canvas and the anchor) are for the SILHOUETTE CHECK and the height reading: never attached to a call. The jsons of this building list no see-through gaps. Check that every textured and bare picture is 1536 x 1024 and that each json names its job (ironworks) and view and the same canvas; if not, stop and say so. Never git add anything from the two folders. Read assets/source/buildings-v2/scaffolds-textured/README.md on origin/claude/art-scaffolds-review-2 (what the renders are and are not, and the notes of this building: the weak places of its texture) and assets/source/buildings-v2/GUIDE.md on develop, section 2 (the rules every picture follows). The pilot TB1 is the model of this prompt: its prompt, its ledger (codex-queue/status/textured-pilot.md) and its report (assets/source/textured-pilot/REPORT.md on origin/art/textured-pilot) are there to read, never to edit.

PAINTING OVER THE TEXTURED RENDER. Every call is an image EDIT of the textured render of its view, in which you repaint EVERY surface into one coherent painting in the game's painted style as the reference does it (crisp pixel-art-inspired clustered shading, broad readable masses, three or four shades per material, restrained detail, a strong silhouette). Fix, specifically: (a) the CLONED patches: a brick, stone or plank wall, a slate roof or a pile of coal or stone must not repeat the same patch, the same chip or the same blotch twice; courses and planks run straight, evenly and continuously across the wall and round the corners; (b) the SMEARED patches: anything stretched, blurred, streaked or muddy is painted again crisply; (c) the SEAMS: the lines and colour steps where two patches meet (on walls, between roof slopes, at quoins, at the foot of a wall) are painted out: one surface, one continuous painting; (d) the BLOCKY hidden sides: the backs of chimneys, stacks, tanks, towers and frames: crisp courses and the same materials and details as the visible sides; (e) the SMEARED round and curved parts (pipes, bends, wheels, drums, tanks, silos, glazing): painted round, with rivets, flanges, spokes, hoops, glazing bars and highlights in the reference's way; (f) the THIN parts (downpipes, gutters, bargeboards, posts, brackets, railings, ladders, ropes, chains, rods, window bars, finials, pots) finished crisply, one clean shape each. Where the reference shows a surface, take its stones, bricks, planks, slates, doors, windows and fittings from it, crisp; where it never showed a surface (the back walls, the far roof slopes, the hidden sides), paint it as this same building would have it, in the same hand, from the materials and details of the sides that are known: never soft, never plain, never a copy of the front's patch.
What stays: the render's outline, its camera, its proportions and where every roof, wall, chimney, opening, post, pipe and part stands and at what size. Where the render is see-through (a gap between the legs of a frame, under a beam, in a lattice), the picture stays transparent there. Nothing is added (no new window, door, lamp, sign, plant, crate, step, vent or detail that the render does not carry) and nothing is removed or moved. The picture is checked against the render's silhouette and by eye against the render's layout.
LIGHT. The render's light is the game's: the sun from the upper left, fixed for all views, so that the lower-left wall is the lit one, the lower-right wall the shaded one, roofs and tops lightest. The lit side stays where the render has it, in every view, and a wall that the render shows nearly equal in light stays nearly equal. The reference painting's own shading (it was painted with its own light) is not used where it differs from the render's: take its materials, colours and brushwork, not its light. Do not add cast shadows, glow or a new highlight pattern.

THE BUILDING, what it carries round all its views (the reference is the authority for materials and details; where this text and the render disagree about what stands where, the render is right: paint the render):
- Ironworks (`ironworks`): an ironworks of red brick with cream limestone quoins and sills and teal standing-seam sheet roofs: a tall furnace gable with a glowing arched furnace mouth framed in black iron straps, a black iron trough and stone steps in front of it, a small arched window above it, and a tall square red-brick chimney with a cream band, a black iron band with rivets and a black cap rising behind it; a small lean-to at one end with an arched window, a teal door and a copper boiler with copper pipes; and a long hall with a teal roof carrying a clerestory of small windows on its ridge, whose open side is a timber-framed bay with stacks of grey iron ingots, a hoist with a hanging chain and a timber fence, and whose brick wall has arched windows with cream surrounds.

THE VIEWS (canvas 1536 x 1024, 6 canvas px to a game px). The render of each view is the authority for what stands where; these lines name what it shows (r1 to r3 are r0 turned clockwise seen from above; the lower-left and lower-right sides are the visible ones):
r0: the visible sides are the lower-left and the lower-right. What the building has where: the furnace gable with the glowing furnace mouth, the iron trough, the steps and the small arched window, and beyond it the small lean-to with its teal door and copper boiler (the lower-left side); the open timber-framed bay with the ingot stacks, the hoist and chain and the fence, and the brick wall with its arched windows (the lower-right side); the tall brick chimney with its cream and iron bands (it rises behind the furnace gable) (the left corner); the long teal roof with the clerestory of small windows on its ridge (the far (top) corner).
r1: the building is turned 1 quarter turn clockwise seen from above, about the middle of its footprint, not mirrored; every part keeps its place on the building and moves with it, so: the furnace gable with the glowing furnace mouth, the iron trough, the steps and the small arched window, and beyond it the small lean-to with its teal door and copper boiler stands at the upper-left side; the open timber-framed bay with the ingot stacks, the hoist and chain and the fence, and the brick wall with its arched windows stands at the lower-left side; the tall brick chimney with its cream and iron bands (it rises behind the furnace gable) stands at the far (top) corner; the long teal roof with the clerestory of small windows on its ridge stands at the right corner. What stands on the upper sides is hidden behind the building except where the render shows it.
r2: the building is turned 2 quarter turns (a half turn) clockwise seen from above, about the middle of its footprint, not mirrored; every part keeps its place on the building and moves with it, so: the furnace gable with the glowing furnace mouth, the iron trough, the steps and the small arched window, and beyond it the small lean-to with its teal door and copper boiler stands at the upper-right side; the open timber-framed bay with the ingot stacks, the hoist and chain and the fence, and the brick wall with its arched windows stands at the upper-left side; the tall brick chimney with its cream and iron bands (it rises behind the furnace gable) stands at the right corner; the long teal roof with the clerestory of small windows on its ridge stands at the near (bottom) corner. What stands on the upper sides is hidden behind the building except where the render shows it.
r3: the building is turned 3 quarter turns clockwise seen from above, about the middle of its footprint, not mirrored; every part keeps its place on the building and moves with it, so: the furnace gable with the glowing furnace mouth, the iron trough, the steps and the small arched window, and beyond it the small lean-to with its teal door and copper boiler stands at the lower-right side; the open timber-framed bay with the ingot stacks, the hoist and chain and the fence, and the brick wall with its arched windows stands at the upper-right side; the tall brick chimney with its cream and iron bands (it rises behind the furnace gable) stands at the near (bottom) corner; the long teal roof with the clerestory of small windows on its ridge stands at the left corner. What stands on the upper sides is hidden behind the building except where the render shows it.

THE SIZE, a measurement and not a test. Art scales a returned picture UNIFORMLY at packing, one factor on both axes and no shear: paint at the size the render has, and never resize, crop, move, stretch, squeeze, shear, rotate or bend a returned picture yourself. Record the base's width (opaque width of the drawn base at ground level, from the outer edge of the left corner to the outer edge of the right corner, outline included; chimneys, canopies, pipes, towers and anything above the ground do not count; where the tool puts a corner on a post or a pipe, read the outer corner by eye on a zoomed crop and record both values), in px of the returned picture, and k = the render's base width (from its json: model_base_px, w to e, in canvas px, times returned width / canvas width) divided by that width.

THE ORDER AND THE REFERENCES. Paint in the order of the first paragraph: ironworks r0, ironworks r1, ironworks r2, ironworks r3. One call is one attempt of one view. A view starts only after the last one is measured and recorded. Every call is an image edit of the view's textured render, attached first, then, in this order and never more:
  r0: (1) ../scaffolds-textured-b/ironworks-r0-textured.png, the picture to paint over; (2) ../originals-textured-b/ironworks.png, the original painting, the reference for detail, colour and brushwork.
  r1, r2, r3: (1) ../scaffolds-textured-b/ironworks-r<v>-textured.png, the picture to paint over; (2) ../originals-textured-b/ironworks.png, the original painting; (3) assets/source/textured/ironworks/ironworks-r0.png, this prompt's accepted r0 (attach it only if r0 was accepted; if r0 was refused, attach the original alone). The third picture is for consistency only: the same materials, colours and brushwork as the accepted r0, in a different view; it is not a pattern to copy.
Never attach a render of another view, a bare render, a json, an earlier attempt of yours, an earlier painting of another prompt or route, or a picture that failed.

THE PICTURE PROMPT, word for word, on EVERY attempt, the first included (then at most one Correction paragraph, below). It has three parts, in this order: PART A, the same for every view; PART B, the building's and the view's own; PART C, the same for every view.

PART A:

"Edit the first picture, a textured 3D render of a building, into ONE finished painting for a pastoral isometric railway game: the building named under View, the SAME building as in the second picture, in the view named under View. The first picture has the right shape, camera, proportions, layout and light, but its texture has fallen apart in places: cloned and mirrored patches that repeat the same bricks, stones or planks, smeared and stretched areas, seams where patches meet, blocky hidden sides, smeared pipes and round parts, blurred glazing and thin parts. Paint over it, surface by surface, so that the whole building becomes ONE coherent painting in the style and materials of the second picture, the building's original painting: take its colours, brushwork, level of detail and crispness (the way its bricks, stones, planks, slates, glazing, iron and fittings are painted) and apply them to every wall, roof, chimney and part of the first picture, including the sides the second picture never shows, which you paint as this same building would have them, in the same hand. Remove the cloned and smeared patches and the seams, paint the hidden sides crisp, paint the pipes, wheels, tanks and glazing round and crisp, and finish the thin parts (downpipes, gutters, bargeboards, posts, brackets, railings, ladders, ropes, window bars, finials, pots) as clean shapes. Keep, exactly as the first picture has them: the silhouette, the camera, the proportions and the position and size of every roof, wall, chimney, window, door, post, pipe and part, and every see-through gap; add nothing (no new window, door, lamp, sign, plant, crate, step or detail) and remove nothing. Light: the first picture's light is the game's sun from the upper left and stays as it is: the lit wall stays lit, the shaded wall stays shaded, roofs and tops lightest. The palette family of the whole game: forest green #294638, moss #78804C, oat cream #E4D5B5, limestone #BCA98B, slate #626B6C, teal #477B82, copper #A36B42. Nothing at or above cursor white #ECE4D0 and nothing as strong as selection amber #E8AA48. The one exception to the amber rule is the glow of the furnace mouth, which stays as the first picture has it (no more of it, and nothing glowing anywhere else).

Camera: keep the first picture's camera exactly: orthographic 2:1 isometric, 30 degrees down, ground edges two pixels across for each pixel down, vertical edges vertical, no perspective. Do not rotate, tilt, turn, resize, shift or reshape the building: its silhouette stays where the first picture's is, and the picture is checked against it. Show the whole building with the same margin all round as the first picture.

Ground: paint only the building. No plinth, ground, floor slab, grass, shadow or pad under it that the first picture does not have; keep the foot of the walls plain and straight where the first picture has it, with nothing added in front of it."

PART B (the same shape for each view: the BUILDING paragraph of THE BUILDING above (its bullet, from "an ironworks of red" to the end of the bullet), then the VIEW paragraph of THE VIEWS above for that view, then the references paragraph). Write it out whole in each call, in this form:

"View. The building is the Ironworks in the view [r0 | r1 | r2 | r3]. [the BUILDING paragraph, word for word from THE BUILDING] [the view's line, word for word from THE VIEWS, from "r<v>:" to the end of that line] Every part the first picture shows stands where the first picture puts it: paint it as shown, and do not add what the first picture does not carry.

References: the first is the textured render, the picture to paint over: its shape, angle, outline, proportions, light and layout do not change. The second is the original painting of this building as the game has it today, seen from the front at a slightly different camera on a larger canvas: take its materials, colours, brushwork and details from it and paint them over the first picture, surface by surface; its angle, canvas, footprint, shading and any dark glow round it are not used, and it is not to be copied: paint the SAME building at the size the first picture has it. The third is this same building in view r0, as already painted over its render (the kept picture): the same materials, colours and brushwork must carry through, in this other view; it is not a pattern to copy and its angle is not this picture's. [the references paragraph, in the form above for the view: for r0 without the sentence about the third picture, for r1, r2 and r3 with it]"

PART C:

"Do not paint the plinth itself: no ground, floor slab, pavement, grass or shadow under or around the building. A real RGBA PNG with a transparent background (alpha zero) and opaque building surfaces, and transparent wherever the first picture is see-through. Never: rails, trains, people, vehicles, smoke, lettering, signs with text, borders, glow, or loose objects standing apart from the building."

THE MEASURING SCRIPT. Your lane's script is assets/source/textured/measure-textured-b.mjs. If it does not exist yet (this is the first prompt of the group), create it from the pilot's: git show origin/art/textured-pilot:assets/source/textured-pilot/measure-textured.mjs (read it first; copy it, never edit the pilot's), adapted to this prompt: folders ../scaffolds-textured-b (renders, jsons) and ../originals-textured-b; the building and the canvas from the command line; the thresholds of THE TESTS below (IoU minimum per building); the see-through gaps of the json (`outline_holes_px` or `hole_polygons_px`, whichever the json has) in the silhouette; and the scale of the canvas (canvas px per game px: 8, 6 for the 2 x 2 buildings, 5 for the power plant). It reads a returned picture's alpha (alpha above 128, with pngjs where tools/building-check.mjs pictureOf gives only metadata) and the view's json and bare render from ../scaffolds-textured-b, and prints, per picture: the returned size and s0 = returned width / canvas width; the silhouette numbers below; the opaque box; the base's width and k (THE SIZE); the height numbers below; the counts of opaque pixels with red, green and blue all at or above #ECE4D0 and within 12 (RGB distance) of #E8AA48, on the picture and on the reference; the mean lightness of the two walls just above their feet (reported); and PASS or FAIL for each machine test below. There is no separate camera, foot-ratio or footprint test: the render carries the camera and the plan, and the silhouette decides. If npm needs installing for the script, run npm ci inside your worktree with npm_config_cache=G:/DEV/wt/_npmcache; never on C:.

THE TESTS. A picture is ACCEPTED when every test passes. The base of the render is the exact shape, so these are stricter than the thresholds of the earlier scaffold prompts.
1. SILHOUETTE (machine). The render's silhouette is the polygon outline_polygon_px of the view's json (canvas px; filled; scaled by s0 to the returned picture). The picture's silhouette is its opaque pixels (alpha above 128) with the enclosed holes filled, opened by 2 canvas px so that antialiasing specks do not count. Compute: (a) the overlap IoU at s0 with no shift, reported; (b) the best uniform fit: ONE factor f between 0.85 and 1.15 on both axes and a shift (dx, dy), chosen to maximise the IoU (a coarse-to-fine search is fine), which is all Art's packing does to a picture; record f, dx, dy and the IoU at the best fit; (c) at the best fit, the distances in canvas px (returned px divided by s0 x f) from every point of the picture's OUTER outline to the render's outer outline, and from every point of the render's outer outline to the picture's (the outlines of the see-through gaps are not in the distance sets, they count in the IoU); the 95th percentile of each of the two sets is the value at rank ceil(0.95 n) of its sorted distances; the maximum of both sets. PASS needs: the IoU at the best fit AT LEAST 0.92 AND the 95th percentile of each set AT MOST 16 canvas px (the larger of the two is the figure) AND the maximum of both sets AT MOST 32 canvas px. Record IoU at s0, f, dx, dy, the best-fit IoU, the maximum and the two 95th percentiles.
2. HEIGHT (machine plus eye). Scaling does not change a proportion, so read it as one: (a) the height of the hall wall under its eaves at the near corner of the hall, over the base's width e.x - w.x: read on the picture and on the same view's bare render the same way (zoomed crops, by eye); (b) the overall height, from the top of the highest opaque pixel to the lowest, over the opaque width of the silhouette (machine, on the picture and on the bare render's alpha). PASS needs both numbers of the picture within 8 per cent of the bare render's.
3. SAME BUILDING (eye, on crops beside the original and the render): the building of the render in this view: every window, door and feature in place and in number (nothing added, nothing missing, nothing moved against the render); the materials, roof form and colours of the reference; the furnace mouth with its fire, the tall chimney with its bands, the clerestory windows, the ingot stacks, the hoist chain, the copper boiler and pipes, the fence.
4. ONE COHERENT PAINTING (eye): no cloned patch (the same chipped brick, plank, stone or blotch repeated), no smeared or stretched area, no visible seam between patches or between roof slopes, crisp hidden sides, round parts painted round and crisp, thin parts finished crisply: side by side with the textured render, every surface is repainted and none was left as the render has it; and side by side with the original, one hand. A picture whose surfaces are merely the render's, sharpened or filtered, fails.
5. LIGHT (eye, on crops): the light side as in the render, in every view: the lower-left wall lighter than the lower-right wall where the render has it so, roofs and tops lightest, and a wall that the render shows nearly equal in light stays nearly equal. Read the render's ratio of the lower-left over the lower-right wall from two patches just above the feet of the two walls and report the picture's ratio beside it. The script's wall lightness is reported and not counted unless the eye confirms it.
6. NO EXTRAS (eye): no plinth, ground, shadow or glow painted; no lettering, people, rails, trains or smoke; nothing orange except what the render has; the facts of the render (what stands where) not changed.
Reported, NOT a pass rule: the highlight counts (tools/atlas-cap.mjs, issue #465, caps both at packing); the base's width and k; the wall lightness. Keep them low; never refuse or stop a picture for them.

MEASURE EVERY PICTURE BEFORE YOU RECORD IT, in this order: silhouette, height, the three eye tests with crops, then the reports. Before the first call of a view, read the bare render once the way the height is read (a zoomed crop) and write the numbers to prompts.json as renderReference.

CORRECTIONS AND STOPS. A picture that fails is made again from the printed prompt: keep PART A, PART B and PART C whole and add under them ONE paragraph that begins "Correction:" and names the test it missed, the number or the place, and where it belongs, as "your last r1 put the top 29 canvas px above the render's outline, and left the back wall's planks cloned in two places": it replaces the earlier Correction paragraph; no prompt has more than one. Always edit the textured render of the view, never an earlier attempt of yours: attach only what THE ORDER AND THE REFERENCES lists. Never resize, crop, move, stretch, squeeze, shear, rotate or bend a returned picture to make it fit: the shape comes from painting over the render. AT MOST THREE ATTEMPTS A VIEW (12 calls in all). If none of a view's attempts passes, keep the closest REFUSED attempt in raw/ (not copied to the building's accepted file), record it in prompts.json with kept: false, the test it failed and its number, and the reason in one sentence, say so in the ledger, and go on to the next view (a failed view is never accepted quietly). STOP RULE, per building: if all three attempts of a view have a silhouette IoU under 0.80 and the same happened to the view before it, stop THIS building after that view: commit what you have, write the ledger entry and say so (the method does not hold the shape: Core decides); the group goes on with its next prompt.

COMMIT AND PUSH. After this building's views are done (accepted or refused), commit its folder (assets/source/textured/ironworks/: the accepted pictures, raw/, prompts.json, and assets/source/textured/measure-textured-b.mjs if it is new or changed) with "Art: textured, ironworks r0 r1 r2 r3 painted over the textured renders" and push the lane, so a stop later never loses a finished building. Look at each kept picture beside its render and the reference (your own scratch under .look/ or ../, not committed, never on C:) and at the views side by side: they should be one building painted in one hand.

In your message, "Views accepted" lists each accepted view with its path and attempt and the deciding numbers (IoU at the best fit, the two 95th percentiles and the maximum, the two height numbers), and "Refused attempts" lists each refused attempt with the test it failed and its number and names the closest kept per failed view in raw/.

REPORT. In your message give, for each attempt: the building and view, the returned size, the silhouette numbers (IoU at s0, f, dx, dy, the IoU at the best fit, the maximum and the two 95th percentiles), the two height numbers against the render's, the base's width and k, the highlight counts, and which attempt was kept; say plainly which views passed every test and which were kept as the closest refused; and, in two or three sentences, your own verdict by eye on the method for this building: did the repainted surfaces read as one coherent painting in the reference's style, over the exact shape, and where did they still fall apart (hidden sides, thin parts, seams, cloned patches)?

PROMPT TB2-oil_derrick (22 October 2026), the textured route after the pilot TB1. Paint 4 pictures for the isometric railway game in this repository: the Oil derrick (pumpjack) (`oil_derrick`), views r0, r1, r2, r3, in this order. Every picture is painted OVER a TEXTURED 3D BLOCKOUT RENDER of that view; the building's ORIGINAL r0 PAINTING is the reference for the look.

WHY. The author tried two earlier ways of getting the turned views: free painting on grey guides (the camera was lost) and painting over AI-reconstructed scaffolds (morphed shapes; held and now superseded). Core then built each building as a clean Blender blockout matched to the painted r0, projected the original painting onto it, and rendered all four views at the game's exact camera with the game's sun: the render's camera, proportions and every part's position are exact. What the render is not is one coherent painting: its back walls and far roof slopes are clones and mirror images (they repeat verbatim and are a little blocky and uniform), the patches meet in seams, and the thin and round parts are smeared by the projection onto plain boxes and cylinders. TB1 tested the method on the warehouse and the water pump: all eight views were accepted (IoU 0.958 to 0.983), the pictures read as one coherent painting in the original's hand. TB1's power plant failed because the full original painting was attached: the tool copied its size, its position and its glow. Keep the same discipline: paint over the render, never copy the reference's size or position.

THE JOB, in one paragraph. Paint over the textured render. Keep its silhouette, camera, proportions and every part's position exactly. Unify its surface into ONE coherent painting in the style and materials of the building's original r0 painting: fix the cloned and smeared patches, the seams and the blocky hidden sides, and finish the thin parts crisply. The lighting follows the game sun as in the render: the lit side stays where the render has it. No new elements, nothing removed.

LANE AND FILES. Branch art/textured-b (above). Touch nothing outside assets/source/textured/oil_derrick/ (one folder, with raw/ inside) and your lane's measuring script assets/source/textured/measure-textured-b.mjs (the first prompt of this group creates it, later prompts of the group reuse and, if they must, extend it; each lane has its own file so that the lanes do not collide). Never push to main or develop. Commit subjects read "Art: <outcome>". Keep every picture your image tool returns, byte for byte, as assets/source/textured/oil_derrick/raw/oil_derrick-r<v>-attempt-<k>.png (v the view, k from 1 for each view; if assets/source/textured/oil_derrick/prompts.json already holds attempts of a view (an earlier run), go on from its highest k and never overwrite or attach one; a view that an earlier run already accepted is not painted again), and record each call in assets/source/textured/oil_derrick/prompts.json: {view, attempt, file, prompt, references (in order), returned (the size it came back), measured (the numbers below), kept, note}. Never delete or overwrite a picture. Never commit the renders or the originals you fetch (below). Ask the tool for the canvas size (1024 x 1024), a transparent background, and record the size it returns (a 1024 px wide canvas has come back 1254 px wide; 1536 x 1024 came back as asked).
Accepted pictures (and only accepted ones) are copied byte for byte from raw/ to assets/source/textured/oil_derrick/oil_derrick-r<v>.png.

THE INPUTS. Make two folders OUTSIDE the repository, on G: next to your worktree: ../scaffolds-textured-b and ../originals-textured-b (they are this group's own; oil_derrick files only). Write the files out of git byte for byte (a PowerShell redirect corrupts a PNG: use bash, or cmd /c, or git cat-file blob with --filters off). From the repository root, in bash:

  git fetch origin claude/art-scaffolds-review-2 develop
  mkdir -p ../scaffolds-textured-b ../originals-textured-b
  for v in 0 1 2 3; do for f in textured.png bare.png; do git show origin/claude/art-scaffolds-review-2:assets/source/buildings-v2/scaffolds-textured/oil_derrick/oil_derrick-r$v-$f > ../scaffolds-textured-b/oil_derrick-r$v-$f; done; git show origin/claude/art-scaffolds-review-2:assets/source/buildings-v2/scaffolds-textured/oil_derrick/oil_derrick-r$v.json > ../scaffolds-textured-b/oil_derrick-r$v.json; done
  git fetch origin develop
  git show origin/develop:assets/source/base-v1/works-full-oil-derrick.png > ../originals-textured-b/oil_derrick.png

The original is the building picture as the game has it today and it stays as it is: origin/develop:assets/source/base-v1/works-full-oil-derrick.png. Check its sha256 first, on the bytes you wrote: a7d6f694fdc2d4c0d72dd3717d77ba7cba8eff69f1ad03b8499b699701ca375c. If it differs, stop and say so. Its size is 1774 x 887; it may have a dark glow or shadow round the building: that is not part of what you paint.
Per view: oil_derrick-r<v>-textured.png is the render to PAINT OVER (variant B of the pilot: the painting's own shading taken out and the model relit with the game's sun, upper left): its shape, camera, proportions, layout, light and colours are right, its surface is not one painting. oil_derrick-r<v>-bare.png (the same shape in plain clay) and oil_derrick-r<v>.json (outline_polygon_px, the silhouette in canvas px; the base and footprint corners; the camera numbers; the canvas and the anchor) are for the SILHOUETTE CHECK and the height reading: never attached to a call. The jsons carry `hole_polygons_px` (the see-through gaps of the silhouette: polygons in canvas px, present for some views, empty or absent for others); they are used by the silhouette test below. Check that every textured and bare picture is 1024 x 1024 and that each json names its job (oil_derrick) and view and the same canvas; if not, stop and say so. Never git add anything from the two folders. Read assets/source/buildings-v2/scaffolds-textured/README.md on origin/claude/art-scaffolds-review-2 (what the renders are and are not, and the notes of this building: the weak places of its texture) and assets/source/buildings-v2/GUIDE.md on develop, section 2 (the rules every picture follows). The pilot TB1 is the model of this prompt: its prompt, its ledger (codex-queue/status/textured-pilot.md) and its report (assets/source/textured-pilot/REPORT.md on origin/art/textured-pilot) are there to read, never to edit.

PAINTING OVER THE TEXTURED RENDER. Every call is an image EDIT of the textured render of its view, in which you repaint EVERY surface into one coherent painting in the game's painted style as the reference does it (crisp pixel-art-inspired clustered shading, broad readable masses, three or four shades per material, restrained detail, a strong silhouette). Fix, specifically: (a) the CLONED patches: a brick, stone or plank wall, a slate roof or a pile of coal or stone must not repeat the same patch, the same chip or the same blotch twice; courses and planks run straight, evenly and continuously across the wall and round the corners; (b) the SMEARED patches: anything stretched, blurred, streaked or muddy is painted again crisply; (c) the SEAMS: the lines and colour steps where two patches meet (on walls, between roof slopes, at quoins, at the foot of a wall) are painted out: one surface, one continuous painting; (d) the BLOCKY hidden sides: the backs of chimneys, stacks, tanks, towers and frames: crisp courses and the same materials and details as the visible sides; (e) the SMEARED round and curved parts (pipes, bends, wheels, drums, tanks, silos, glazing): painted round, with rivets, flanges, spokes, hoops, glazing bars and highlights in the reference's way; (f) the THIN parts (downpipes, gutters, bargeboards, posts, brackets, railings, ladders, ropes, chains, rods, window bars, finials, pots) finished crisply, one clean shape each. Where the reference shows a surface, take its stones, bricks, planks, slates, doors, windows and fittings from it, crisp; where it never showed a surface (the back walls, the far roof slopes, the hidden sides), paint it as this same building would have it, in the same hand, from the materials and details of the sides that are known: never soft, never plain, never a copy of the front's patch.
What stays: the render's outline, its camera, its proportions and where every roof, wall, chimney, opening, post, pipe and part stands and at what size. Where the render is see-through (a gap between the legs of a frame, under a beam, in a lattice), the picture stays transparent there. Nothing is added (no new window, door, lamp, sign, plant, crate, step, vent or detail that the render does not carry) and nothing is removed or moved. The picture is checked against the render's silhouette and by eye against the render's layout. This building is mostly thin steel. Keep every lattice bar, rod, pipe and the beam thin and crisp, exactly where the render has it, and keep the see-through gaps transparent; the horse head is a plate with openings, paint its back as crisply as its front.
LIGHT. The render's light is the game's: the sun from the upper left, fixed for all views, so that the lower-left wall is the lit one, the lower-right wall the shaded one, roofs and tops lightest. The lit side stays where the render has it, in every view, and a wall that the render shows nearly equal in light stays nearly equal. The reference painting's own shading (it was painted with its own light) is not used where it differs from the render's: take its materials, colours and brushwork, not its light. Do not add cast shadows, glow or a new highlight pattern.

THE BUILDING, what it carries round all its views (the reference is the authority for materials and details; where this text and the render disagree about what stands where, the render is right: paint the render):
- Oil derrick (pumpjack) (`oil_derrick`): an oil pumpjack of dark grey riveted steel with weathered copper: a lattice A-frame Samson post of X-braced steel legs with rivets, carrying a long walking beam with a copper top edge, which ends at one side in a big curved copper-edged horse head with drilled openings and at the other in a pivot; a polished rod running down from the horse head through a flanged wellhead with copper rings; a grey cylindrical tank with copper bands at one end of the base; a crank, a spoked copper-rimmed flywheel and a rod on a steel engine box; copper pipes with elbows along the steel base frame; and the steel skid frame with corner plates and rivets that carries it all.

THE VIEWS (canvas 1024 x 1024, 8 canvas px to a game px). The render of each view is the authority for what stands where; these lines name what it shows (r1 to r3 are r0 turned clockwise seen from above; the lower-left and lower-right sides are the visible ones):
r0: the visible sides are the lower-left and the lower-right. What the building has where: the grey cylindrical tank with copper bands and the crank with its spoked flywheel on the steel engine box (the left corner); the lattice Samson post with its pivot, and the walking beam that rests on it (the far (top) corner); the curved horse head, the polished rod and the flanged wellhead with its copper rings and pipes (the right corner); the long steel skid frame with its copper pipes along that side (the lower-left side).
r1: the building is turned 1 quarter turn clockwise seen from above, about the middle of its footprint, not mirrored; every part keeps its place on the building and moves with it, so: the grey cylindrical tank with copper bands and the crank with its spoked flywheel on the steel engine box stands at the far (top) corner; the lattice Samson post with its pivot, and the walking beam that rests on it stands at the right corner; the curved horse head, the polished rod and the flanged wellhead with its copper rings and pipes stands at the near (bottom) corner; the long steel skid frame with its copper pipes along that side stands at the upper-left side. What stands on the upper sides is hidden behind the building except where the render shows it.
r2: the building is turned 2 quarter turns (a half turn) clockwise seen from above, about the middle of its footprint, not mirrored; every part keeps its place on the building and moves with it, so: the grey cylindrical tank with copper bands and the crank with its spoked flywheel on the steel engine box stands at the right corner; the lattice Samson post with its pivot, and the walking beam that rests on it stands at the near (bottom) corner; the curved horse head, the polished rod and the flanged wellhead with its copper rings and pipes stands at the left corner; the long steel skid frame with its copper pipes along that side stands at the upper-right side. What stands on the upper sides is hidden behind the building except where the render shows it.
r3: the building is turned 3 quarter turns clockwise seen from above, about the middle of its footprint, not mirrored; every part keeps its place on the building and moves with it, so: the grey cylindrical tank with copper bands and the crank with its spoked flywheel on the steel engine box stands at the near (bottom) corner; the lattice Samson post with its pivot, and the walking beam that rests on it stands at the left corner; the curved horse head, the polished rod and the flanged wellhead with its copper rings and pipes stands at the far (top) corner; the long steel skid frame with its copper pipes along that side stands at the lower-right side. What stands on the upper sides is hidden behind the building except where the render shows it.

THE SIZE, a measurement and not a test. Art scales a returned picture UNIFORMLY at packing, one factor on both axes and no shear: paint at the size the render has, and never resize, crop, move, stretch, squeeze, shear, rotate or bend a returned picture yourself. Record the base's width (opaque width of the drawn base at ground level, from the outer edge of the left corner to the outer edge of the right corner, outline included; chimneys, canopies, pipes, towers and anything above the ground do not count; where the tool puts a corner on a post or a pipe, read the outer corner by eye on a zoomed crop and record both values), in px of the returned picture, and k = the render's base width (from its json: model_base_px, w to e, in canvas px, times returned width / canvas width) divided by that width.

THE ORDER AND THE REFERENCES. Paint in the order of the first paragraph: oil_derrick r0, oil_derrick r1, oil_derrick r2, oil_derrick r3. One call is one attempt of one view. A view starts only after the last one is measured and recorded. Every call is an image edit of the view's textured render, attached first, then, in this order and never more:
  r0: (1) ../scaffolds-textured-b/oil_derrick-r0-textured.png, the picture to paint over; (2) ../originals-textured-b/oil_derrick.png, the original painting, the reference for detail, colour and brushwork.
  r1, r2, r3: (1) ../scaffolds-textured-b/oil_derrick-r<v>-textured.png, the picture to paint over; (2) ../originals-textured-b/oil_derrick.png, the original painting; (3) assets/source/textured/oil_derrick/oil_derrick-r0.png, this prompt's accepted r0 (attach it only if r0 was accepted; if r0 was refused, attach the original alone). The third picture is for consistency only: the same materials, colours and brushwork as the accepted r0, in a different view; it is not a pattern to copy.
Never attach a render of another view, a bare render, a json, an earlier attempt of yours, an earlier painting of another prompt or route, or a picture that failed.

THE PICTURE PROMPT, word for word, on EVERY attempt, the first included (then at most one Correction paragraph, below). It has three parts, in this order: PART A, the same for every view; PART B, the building's and the view's own; PART C, the same for every view.

PART A:

"Edit the first picture, a textured 3D render of a building, into ONE finished painting for a pastoral isometric railway game: the building named under View, the SAME building as in the second picture, in the view named under View. The first picture has the right shape, camera, proportions, layout and light, but its texture has fallen apart in places: cloned and mirrored patches that repeat the same bricks, stones or planks, smeared and stretched areas, seams where patches meet, blocky hidden sides, smeared pipes and round parts, blurred glazing and thin parts. Paint over it, surface by surface, so that the whole building becomes ONE coherent painting in the style and materials of the second picture, the building's original painting: take its colours, brushwork, level of detail and crispness (the way its bricks, stones, planks, slates, glazing, iron and fittings are painted) and apply them to every wall, roof, chimney and part of the first picture, including the sides the second picture never shows, which you paint as this same building would have them, in the same hand. Remove the cloned and smeared patches and the seams, paint the hidden sides crisp, paint the pipes, wheels, tanks and glazing round and crisp, and finish the thin parts (downpipes, gutters, bargeboards, posts, brackets, railings, ladders, ropes, window bars, finials, pots) as clean shapes. Keep, exactly as the first picture has them: the silhouette, the camera, the proportions and the position and size of every roof, wall, chimney, window, door, post, pipe and part, and every see-through gap; add nothing (no new window, door, lamp, sign, plant, crate, step or detail) and remove nothing. Light: the first picture's light is the game's sun from the upper left and stays as it is: the lit wall stays lit, the shaded wall stays shaded, roofs and tops lightest. The palette family of the whole game: forest green #294638, moss #78804C, oat cream #E4D5B5, limestone #BCA98B, slate #626B6C, teal #477B82, copper #A36B42. Nothing at or above cursor white #ECE4D0 and nothing as strong as selection amber #E8AA48.

Camera: keep the first picture's camera exactly: orthographic 2:1 isometric, 30 degrees down, ground edges two pixels across for each pixel down, vertical edges vertical, no perspective. Do not rotate, tilt, turn, resize, shift or reshape the building: its silhouette stays where the first picture's is, and the picture is checked against it. Show the whole building with the same margin all round as the first picture.

Ground: paint only the building. No plinth, ground, floor slab, grass, shadow or pad under it that the first picture does not have; keep the foot of the walls plain and straight where the first picture has it, with nothing added in front of it."

PART B (the same shape for each view: the BUILDING paragraph of THE BUILDING above (its bullet, from "an oil pumpjack of" to the end of the bullet), then the VIEW paragraph of THE VIEWS above for that view, then the references paragraph). Write it out whole in each call, in this form:

"View. The building is the Oil derrick (pumpjack) in the view [r0 | r1 | r2 | r3]. [the BUILDING paragraph, word for word from THE BUILDING] [the view's line, word for word from THE VIEWS, from "r<v>:" to the end of that line] Every part the first picture shows stands where the first picture puts it: paint it as shown, and do not add what the first picture does not carry.

References: the first is the textured render, the picture to paint over: its shape, angle, outline, proportions, light and layout do not change. The second is the original painting of this building as the game has it today, seen from the front at a slightly different camera on a larger canvas: take its materials, colours, brushwork and details from it and paint them over the first picture, surface by surface; its angle, canvas, footprint, shading and any dark glow round it are not used, and it is not to be copied: paint the SAME building at the size the first picture has it. The third is this same building in view r0, as already painted over its render (the kept picture): the same materials, colours and brushwork must carry through, in this other view; it is not a pattern to copy and its angle is not this picture's. [the references paragraph, in the form above for the view: for r0 without the sentence about the third picture, for r1, r2 and r3 with it]"

PART C:

"Do not paint the plinth itself: no ground, floor slab, pavement, grass or shadow under or around the building. A real RGBA PNG with a transparent background (alpha zero) and opaque building surfaces, and transparent wherever the first picture is see-through. Never: rails, trains, people, vehicles, smoke, lettering, signs with text, borders, glow, or loose objects standing apart from the building."

THE MEASURING SCRIPT. Your lane's script is assets/source/textured/measure-textured-b.mjs. If it does not exist yet (this is the first prompt of the group), create it from the pilot's: git show origin/art/textured-pilot:assets/source/textured-pilot/measure-textured.mjs (read it first; copy it, never edit the pilot's), adapted to this prompt: folders ../scaffolds-textured-b (renders, jsons) and ../originals-textured-b; the building and the canvas from the command line; the thresholds of THE TESTS below (IoU minimum per building); the see-through gaps of the json (`outline_holes_px` or `hole_polygons_px`, whichever the json has) in the silhouette; and the scale of the canvas (canvas px per game px: 8, 6 for the 2 x 2 buildings, 5 for the power plant). It reads a returned picture's alpha (alpha above 128, with pngjs where tools/building-check.mjs pictureOf gives only metadata) and the view's json and bare render from ../scaffolds-textured-b, and prints, per picture: the returned size and s0 = returned width / canvas width; the silhouette numbers below; the opaque box; the base's width and k (THE SIZE); the height numbers below; the counts of opaque pixels with red, green and blue all at or above #ECE4D0 and within 12 (RGB distance) of #E8AA48, on the picture and on the reference; the mean lightness of the two walls just above their feet (reported); and PASS or FAIL for each machine test below. There is no separate camera, foot-ratio or footprint test: the render carries the camera and the plan, and the silhouette decides. If npm needs installing for the script, run npm ci inside your worktree with npm_config_cache=G:/DEV/wt/_npmcache; never on C:.

THE TESTS. A picture is ACCEPTED when every test passes. The base of the render is the exact shape, so these are stricter than the thresholds of the earlier scaffold prompts.
1. SILHOUETTE (machine). The render's silhouette is the polygon outline_polygon_px of the view's json (canvas px; filled) MINUS the polygons of hole_polygons_px of the same json (the see-through gaps; none for some views). The picture's silhouette is its opaque pixels (alpha above 128), opened by 2 canvas px so that antialiasing specks do not count; enclosed holes of under 60 canvas px (scaled) area are filled, larger enclosed holes are kept (a gap the render has must stay open in the picture, and a gap that the render does not have counts against the IoU). Both are scaled to the returned picture by s0. Compute: (a) the overlap IoU at s0 with no shift, reported; (b) the best uniform fit: ONE factor f between 0.85 and 1.15 on both axes and a shift (dx, dy), chosen to maximise the IoU (a coarse-to-fine search is fine), which is all Art's packing does to a picture; record f, dx, dy and the IoU at the best fit; (c) at the best fit, the distances in canvas px (returned px divided by s0 x f) from every point of the picture's OUTER outline to the render's outer outline, and from every point of the render's outer outline to the picture's (the outlines of the see-through gaps are not in the distance sets, they count in the IoU); the 95th percentile of each of the two sets is the value at rank ceil(0.95 n) of its sorted distances; the maximum of both sets. PASS needs: the IoU at the best fit AT LEAST 0.88 (the blockout of this building itself matches its painting only at 0.902 and its lattice is thin, so the bar is 0.88 instead of 0.92; nothing else is relaxed) AND the 95th percentile of each set AT MOST 16 canvas px (the larger of the two is the figure) AND the maximum of both sets AT MOST 32 canvas px. Record IoU at s0, f, dx, dy, the best-fit IoU, the maximum and the two 95th percentiles.
2. HEIGHT (machine plus eye). Scaling does not change a proportion, so read it as one: (a) the height of the Samson post, from its foot to its pivot, over the base's width e.x - w.x: read on the picture and on the same view's bare render the same way (zoomed crops, by eye); (b) the overall height, from the top of the highest opaque pixel to the lowest, over the opaque width of the silhouette (machine, on the picture and on the bare render's alpha). PASS needs both numbers of the picture within 8 per cent of the bare render's.
3. SAME BUILDING (eye, on crops beside the original and the render): the building of the render in this view: every window, door and feature in place and in number (nothing added, nothing missing, nothing moved against the render); the materials, roof form and colours of the reference; the lattice post, the beam with its horse head, the polished rod and wellhead, the tank, the flywheel with its crank; the see-through gaps in the lattice, the horse head and the base frame stay see-through as in the render.
4. ONE COHERENT PAINTING (eye): no cloned patch (the same chipped brick, plank, stone or blotch repeated), no smeared or stretched area, no visible seam between patches or between roof slopes, crisp hidden sides, round parts painted round and crisp, thin parts finished crisply: side by side with the textured render, every surface is repainted and none was left as the render has it; and side by side with the original, one hand. A picture whose surfaces are merely the render's, sharpened or filtered, fails.
5. LIGHT (eye, on crops): the light side as in the render, in every view: the lower-left wall lighter than the lower-right wall where the render has it so, roofs and tops lightest, and a wall that the render shows nearly equal in light stays nearly equal. Read the render's ratio of the lower-left over the lower-right wall from two patches just above the feet of the two walls and report the picture's ratio beside it. The script's wall lightness is reported and not counted unless the eye confirms it.
6. NO EXTRAS (eye): no plinth, ground, shadow or glow painted; no lettering, people, rails, trains or smoke; nothing orange except what the render has; the facts of the render (what stands where) not changed.
Reported, NOT a pass rule: the highlight counts (tools/atlas-cap.mjs, issue #465, caps both at packing); the base's width and k; the wall lightness. Keep them low; never refuse or stop a picture for them.

MEASURE EVERY PICTURE BEFORE YOU RECORD IT, in this order: silhouette, height, the three eye tests with crops, then the reports. Before the first call of a view, read the bare render once the way the height is read (a zoomed crop) and write the numbers to prompts.json as renderReference.

CORRECTIONS AND STOPS. A picture that fails is made again from the printed prompt: keep PART A, PART B and PART C whole and add under them ONE paragraph that begins "Correction:" and names the test it missed, the number or the place, and where it belongs, as "your last r1 put the top 29 canvas px above the render's outline, and left the back wall's planks cloned in two places": it replaces the earlier Correction paragraph; no prompt has more than one. Always edit the textured render of the view, never an earlier attempt of yours: attach only what THE ORDER AND THE REFERENCES lists. Never resize, crop, move, stretch, squeeze, shear, rotate or bend a returned picture to make it fit: the shape comes from painting over the render. AT MOST THREE ATTEMPTS A VIEW (12 calls in all). If none of a view's attempts passes, keep the closest REFUSED attempt in raw/ (not copied to the building's accepted file), record it in prompts.json with kept: false, the test it failed and its number, and the reason in one sentence, say so in the ledger, and go on to the next view (a failed view is never accepted quietly). STOP RULE, per building: if all three attempts of a view have a silhouette IoU under 0.80 and the same happened to the view before it, stop THIS building after that view: commit what you have, write the ledger entry and say so (the method does not hold the shape: Core decides); the group goes on with its next prompt.

COMMIT AND PUSH. After this building's views are done (accepted or refused), commit its folder (assets/source/textured/oil_derrick/: the accepted pictures, raw/, prompts.json, and assets/source/textured/measure-textured-b.mjs if it is new or changed) with "Art: textured, oil_derrick r0 r1 r2 r3 painted over the textured renders" and push the lane, so a stop later never loses a finished building. Look at each kept picture beside its render and the reference (your own scratch under .look/ or ../, not committed, never on C:) and at the views side by side: they should be one building painted in one hand.

In your message, "Views accepted" lists each accepted view with its path and attempt and the deciding numbers (IoU at the best fit, the two 95th percentiles and the maximum, the two height numbers), and "Refused attempts" lists each refused attempt with the test it failed and its number and names the closest kept per failed view in raw/.

REPORT. In your message give, for each attempt: the building and view, the returned size, the silhouette numbers (IoU at s0, f, dx, dy, the IoU at the best fit, the maximum and the two 95th percentiles), the two height numbers against the render's, the base's width and k, the highlight counts, and which attempt was kept; say plainly which views passed every test and which were kept as the closest refused; and, in two or three sentences, your own verdict by eye on the method for this building: did the repainted surfaces read as one coherent painting in the reference's style, over the exact shape, and where did they still fall apart (hidden sides, thin parts, seams, cloned patches)?
