The reference for this building is its free r0 painting: origin/art/325-buildings-v2:assets/source/buildings-v2/depot/depot-a0-r0.png. Check its sha256 first, on the bytes you wrote: 5799b429741a9fe3bf01d601225d9d17fb570be1cafa48656349eddd57131331. If it differs, stop and say so. Its size is 1536 x 1024. The painting was made at a camera of about 34.5 degrees (the game's is 30), so it is repainted over the textured render too (r0), together with r2, whose painting is not a rotation of it (best uniform-fit overlap with the blockout 0.879, the bar is 0.88). The accepted r1 and r3 (overlap 0.920 and 0.898) stay as they are and are NOT repainted, touched or used as references.
Per view: depot-r<v>-textured.png is the render to PAINT OVER (variant B of the pilot: the painting's own shading taken out and the model relit with the game's sun, upper left): its shape, camera, proportions, layout, light and colours are right, its surface is not one painting. depot-r<v>-bare.png (the same shape in plain clay) and depot-r<v>.json (outline_polygon_px, the silhouette in canvas px; the base and footprint corners; the camera numbers; the canvas and the anchor) are for the SILHOUETTE CHECK and the height reading: never attached to a call. The pilot TB1 is the model of this prompt: its prompt, its ledger (codex-queue/status/textured-pilot.md) and its report (assets/source/textured-pilot/REPORT.md on origin/art/textured-pilot) are there to read, never to edit.

PAINTING OVER THE TEXTURED RENDER. Every call is an image EDIT of the textured render of its view, in which you repaint EVERY surface into one coherent painting in the game's painted style as the reference does it (crisp pixel-art-inspired clustered shading, broad readable masses, three or four shades per material, restrained detail, a strong silhouette). Fix, specifically: (a) the CLONED patches: a brick, stone or plank wall, a slate roof or a pile of coal or stone must not repeat the same patch, the same chip or the same blotch twice; courses and planks run straight, evenly and continuously across the wall and round the corners; (b) the SMEARED patches: anything stretched, blurred, streaked or muddy is painted again crisply; (c) the SEAMS: the lines and colour steps where two patches meet (on walls, between roof slopes, at quoins, at the foot of a wall) are painted out: one surface, one continuous painting; (d) the BLOCKY hidden sides: the backs of chimneys, stacks, tanks, towers and frames: crisp courses and the same materials and details as the visible sides; (e) the SMEARED round and curved parts (pipes, bends, wheels, drums, tanks, silos, glazing): painted round, with rivets, flanges, spokes, hoops, glazing bars and highlights in the reference's way; (f) the THIN parts (downpipes, gutters, bargeboards, posts, brackets, railings, ladders, ropes, chains, rods, window bars, finials, pots) finished crisply, one clean shape each. Where the reference shows a surface, take its stones, bricks, planks, slates, doors, windows and fittings from it, crisp; where it never showed a surface (the back walls, the far roof slopes, the hidden sides), paint it as this same building would have it, in the same hand, from the materials and details of the sides that are known: never soft, never plain, never a copy of the front's patch.
What stays: the render's outline, its camera, its proportions and where every roof, wall, chimney, opening, post, pipe and part stands and at what size. Where the render is see-through (a gap between the legs of a frame, under a beam, in a lattice), the picture stays transparent there. Nothing is added (no new window, door, lamp, sign, plant, crate, step, vent or detail that the render does not carry) and nothing is removed or moved. The picture is checked against the render's silhouette and by eye against the render's layout.
LIGHT. The render's light is the game's: the sun from the upper left, fixed for all views, so that the lower-left wall is the lit one, the lower-right wall the shaded one, roofs and tops lightest. The lit side stays where the render has it, in every view, and a wall that the render shows nearly equal in light stays nearly equal. The reference painting's own shading (it was painted with its own light) is not used where it differs from the render's: take its materials, colours and brushwork, not its light. Do not add cast shadows, glow or a new highlight pattern.

THE BUILDING, what it carries round all its views (the reference is the authority for materials and details; where this text and the render disagree about what stands where, the render is right: paint the render):
- Depot (`depot`): a main-line two-track engine shed of red brick with cream stone quoins, plinth and arch rings under a grey slate roof with two glazed monitors (rows of small windows in green frames) on its ridge, cast-iron chimneys with pots at the gable corners and a stepped gable on each end; each end wall has two tall arched portals with cream stone rings and green plank doors standing open, through which the hall's inner brick wall shows in shadow above a dark floor; the long side walls carry arched windows with cream surrounds, a green door and grey downpipes.

THE VIEWS (canvas 1536 x 1024, 6 canvas px to a game px). The render of each view is the authority for what stands where; these lines name what it shows (r1 to r3 are r0 turned clockwise seen from above; the lower-left and lower-right sides are the visible ones):
r0: the visible sides are the lower-left and the lower-right. What the building has where: the long side wall with its arched windows with cream surrounds, the green door and the grey downpipes (the lower-left side); the end wall with the two tall arched portals, their cream stone rings and the open green plank doors, under the stepped gable with its chimneys (the lower-right side); the long back wall (brick with downpipes; as the render has it) (the upper-right side); the other end wall with its two portals (open recesses with no door leaves, as the render has them) (the upper-left side).
r2: the building is turned 2 quarter turns (a half turn) clockwise seen from above, about the middle of its footprint, not mirrored; every part keeps its place on the building and moves with it, so: the long side wall with its arched windows with cream surrounds, the green door and the grey downpipes stands at the upper-right side; the end wall with the two tall arched portals, their cream stone rings and the open green plank doors, under the stepped gable with its chimneys stands at the upper-left side; the long back wall (brick with downpipes; as the render has it) stands at the lower-left side; the other end wall with its two portals (open recesses with no door leaves, as the render has them) stands at the lower-right side. What stands on the upper sides is hidden behind the building except where the render shows it.

THE SIZE, a measurement and not a test. Art scales a returned picture UNIFORMLY at packing, one factor on both axes and no shear: paint at the size the render has, and never resize, crop, move, stretch, squeeze, shear, rotate or bend a returned picture yourself. Record the base's width (opaque width of the drawn base at ground level, from the outer edge of the left corner to the outer edge of the right corner, outline included; chimneys, canopies, pipes, towers and anything above the ground do not count; where the tool puts a corner on a post or a pipe, read the outer corner by eye on a zoomed crop and record both values), in px of the returned picture, and k = the render's base width (from its json: model_base_px, w to e, in canvas px, times returned width / canvas width) divided by that width.

THE ORDER AND THE REFERENCES. Paint in the order of the first paragraph: depot r0, depot r2. One call is one attempt of one view. A view starts only after the last one is measured and recorded. Every call is an image edit of the view's textured render, attached first, then, in this order and never more:
  r0: (1) ../scaffolds-textured-d/depot-r0-textured.png, the picture to paint over; (2) ../originals-textured-d/depot-r0.png, the free r0 painting, the reference for detail, colour and brushwork.
  r2: (1) ../scaffolds-textured-d/depot-r2-textured.png, the picture to paint over; (2) ../originals-textured-d/depot-r0.png, the free r0 painting; (3) assets/source/textured/depot/depot-r0.png, this prompt's accepted r0 (attach it only if r0 was accepted; if r0 was refused, attach the free r0 painting alone). The third picture is for consistency only: the same materials, colours and brushwork as the accepted r0, in a different view; it is not a pattern to copy.
Never attach a render of another view, a bare render, a json, an earlier attempt of yours, an earlier painting of another prompt or route, or a picture that failed.

THE PICTURE PROMPT, word for word, on EVERY attempt, the first included (then at most one Correction paragraph, below). It has three parts, in this order: PART A, the same for every view; PART B, the building's and the view's own; PART C, the same for every view.

PART A:

"Edit the first picture, a textured 3D render of a building, into ONE finished painting for a pastoral isometric railway game: the building named under View, the SAME building as in the second picture, in the view named under View. The first picture has the right shape, camera, proportions, layout and light, but its texture has fallen apart in places: cloned and mirrored patches that repeat the same bricks, stones or planks, smeared and stretched areas, seams where patches meet, blocky hidden sides, smeared pipes and round parts, blurred glazing and thin parts. Paint over it, surface by surface, so that the whole building becomes ONE coherent painting in the style and materials of the second picture, the building's free r0 painting: take its colours, brushwork, level of detail and crispness (the way its bricks, stones, planks, slates, glazing, iron and fittings are painted) and apply them to every wall, roof, chimney and part of the first picture, including the sides the second picture never shows, which you paint as this same building would have them, in the same hand. Remove the cloned and smeared patches and the seams, paint the hidden sides crisp, paint the pipes, wheels, tanks and glazing round and crisp, and finish the thin parts (downpipes, gutters, bargeboards, posts, brackets, railings, ladders, ropes, window bars, finials, pots) as clean shapes. Keep, exactly as the first picture has them: the silhouette, the camera, the proportions and the position and size of every roof, wall, chimney, window, door, post, pipe and part, and every see-through gap; add nothing (no new window, door, lamp, sign, plant, crate, step or detail) and remove nothing. Light: the first picture's light is the game's sun from the upper left and stays as it is: the lit wall stays lit, the shaded wall stays shaded, roofs and tops lightest. The palette family of the whole game: forest green #294638, moss #78804C, oat cream #E4D5B5, limestone #BCA98B, slate #626B6C, teal #477B82, copper #A36B42. Nothing at or above cursor white #ECE4D0 and nothing as strong as selection amber #E8AA48.

Camera: keep the first picture's camera exactly: orthographic 2:1 isometric, 30 degrees down, ground edges two pixels across for each pixel down, vertical edges vertical, no perspective. Do not rotate, tilt, turn, resize, shift or reshape the building: its silhouette stays where the first picture's is, and the picture is checked against it. Show the whole building with the same margin all round as the first picture.

Ground: paint only the building. No plinth, ground, floor slab, grass, shadow or pad under it that the first picture does not have; keep the foot of the walls plain and straight where the first picture has it, with nothing added in front of it."

PART B (the same shape for each view: the BUILDING paragraph of THE BUILDING above (its bullet, from "a main-line two-track engine shed" to the end of the bullet), then the VIEW paragraph of THE VIEWS above for that view, then the references paragraph). Write it out whole in each call, in this form:

"View. The building is the Depot in the view [r0 | r2]. [the BUILDING paragraph, word for word from THE BUILDING] [the view's line, word for word from THE VIEWS, from "r<v>:" to the end of that line] Every part the first picture shows stands where the first picture puts it: paint it as shown, and do not add what the first picture does not carry.

References: the first is the textured render, the picture to paint over: its shape, angle, outline, proportions, light and layout do not change. The second is the free r0 painting of this building, seen from the front at a camera of about 34.5 degrees (the game's is 30) on a larger canvas: take its materials, colours, brushwork and details from it and paint them over the first picture, surface by surface; its angle, canvas, footprint, shading and layout of the sides are not used, and it is not to be copied: paint the SAME building at the size the first picture has it. The third is this same building in view r0, as already painted over its render (the kept picture): the same materials, colours and brushwork must carry through, in this other view; it is not a pattern to copy and its angle is not this picture's. [the references paragraph, in the form above for the view: for r0 without the sentence about the third picture, for r2 with it]"

PART C:

"Do not paint the plinth itself: no ground, floor slab, pavement, grass or shadow under or around the building. A real RGBA PNG with a transparent background (alpha zero) and opaque building surfaces, and transparent wherever the first picture is see-through. Never: rails, trains, people, vehicles, smoke, lettering, signs with text, borders, glow, or loose objects standing apart from the building."

THE MEASURING SCRIPT. Your lane's script is assets/source/textured/measure-textured-d.mjs. If it does not exist yet (this is the first prompt of the group), create it from the pilot's: git show origin/art/textured-pilot:assets/source/textured-pilot/measure-textured.mjs (read it first; copy it, never edit the pilot's), adapted to this prompt: folders ../scaffolds-textured-d (renders, jsons) and ../originals-textured-d; the building and the canvas from the command line; the thresholds of THE TESTS below (IoU minimum per building); the see-through gaps of the json (`outline_holes_px` or `hole_polygons_px`, whichever the json has) in the silhouette; and the scale of the canvas (canvas px per game px: 8, 6 for the 2 x 2 buildings, 5 for the power plant). It reads a returned picture's alpha (alpha above 128, with pngjs where tools/building-check.mjs pictureOf gives only metadata) and the view's json and bare render from ../scaffolds-textured-d, and prints, per picture: the returned size and s0 = returned width / canvas width; the silhouette numbers below; the opaque box; the base's width and k (THE SIZE); the height numbers below; the counts of opaque pixels with red, green and blue all at or above #ECE4D0 and within 12 (RGB distance) of #E8AA48, on the picture and on the reference; the mean lightness of the two walls just above their feet (reported); and PASS or FAIL for each machine test below. There is no separate camera, foot-ratio or footprint test: the render carries the camera and the plan, and the silhouette decides. If npm needs installing for the script, run npm ci inside your worktree with npm_config_cache=G:/DEV/wt/_npmcache; never on C:.

THE TESTS. A picture is ACCEPTED when every test passes. The base of the render is the exact shape, so these are stricter than the thresholds of the earlier scaffold prompts.
1. SILHOUETTE (machine). The render's silhouette is the polygon outline_polygon_px of the view's json (canvas px; filled; scaled by s0 to the returned picture). The picture's silhouette is its opaque pixels (alpha above 128) with the enclosed holes filled, opened by 2 canvas px so that antialiasing specks do not count. Compute: (a) the overlap IoU at s0 with no shift, reported; (b) the best uniform fit: ONE factor f between 0.85 and 1.15 on both axes and a shift (dx, dy), chosen to maximise the IoU (a coarse-to-fine search is fine), which is all Art's packing does to a picture; record f, dx, dy and the IoU at the best fit; (c) at the best fit, the distances in canvas px (returned px divided by s0 x f) from every point of the picture's OUTER outline to the render's outer outline, and from every point of the render's outer outline to the picture's (the outlines of the see-through gaps are not in the distance sets, they count in the IoU); the 95th percentile of each of the two sets is the value at rank ceil(0.95 n) of its sorted distances; the maximum of both sets. PASS needs: the IoU at the best fit AT LEAST 0.92 AND the 95th percentile of each set AT MOST 16 canvas px (the larger of the two is the figure) AND the maximum of both sets AT MOST 32 canvas px. Record IoU at s0, f, dx, dy, the best-fit IoU, the maximum and the two 95th percentiles.
2. HEIGHT (machine plus eye). Scaling does not change a proportion, so read it as one: (a) the height of the wall under the eaves at the near corner s: read on the picture and on the same view's bare render the same way (zoomed crops, by eye); (b) the overall height, from the top of the highest opaque pixel to the lowest, over the opaque width of the silhouette (machine, on the picture and on the bare render's alpha). PASS needs both numbers of the picture within 8 per cent of the bare render's.
3. SAME BUILDING (eye, on crops beside the free r0 painting and the render): the building of the render in this view: every window, door and feature in place and in number (nothing added, nothing missing, nothing moved against the render); the materials, roof form and colours of the reference; the two portals of each end with their stone rings and the open green door leaves where the render has them, the two glazed monitors on the ridge with their window bars, the chimneys with their pots, the arched windows and the green door, the downpipes.
4. ONE COHERENT PAINTING (eye): no cloned patch (the same chipped brick, plank, stone or blotch repeated), no smeared or stretched area, no visible seam between patches or between roof slopes, crisp hidden sides, round parts painted round and crisp, thin parts finished crisply: side by side with the textured render, every surface is repainted and none was left as the render has it; and side by side with the free r0 painting, one hand. A picture whose surfaces are merely the render's, sharpened or filtered, fails.
5. LIGHT (eye, on crops): the light side as in the render, in every view: the lower-left wall lighter than the lower-right wall where the render has it so, roofs and tops lightest, and a wall that the render shows nearly equal in light stays nearly equal. Read the render's ratio of the lower-left over the lower-right wall from two patches just above the feet of the two walls and report the picture's ratio beside it. The script's wall lightness is reported and not counted unless the eye confirms it.
6. NO EXTRAS (eye): no plinth, ground, shadow or glow painted; no lettering, people, rails, trains or smoke; nothing orange except what the render has; the facts of the render (what stands where) not changed.
Reported, NOT a pass rule: the highlight counts (tools/atlas-cap.mjs, issue #465, caps both at packing); the base's width and k; the wall lightness. Keep them low; never refuse or stop a picture for them.

MEASURE EVERY PICTURE BEFORE YOU RECORD IT, in this order: silhouette, height, the three eye tests with crops, then the reports. Before the first call of a view, read the bare render once the way the height is read (a zoomed crop) and write the numbers to prompts.json as renderReference.

CORRECTIONS AND STOPS. A picture that fails is made again from the printed prompt: keep PART A, PART B and PART C whole and add under them ONE paragraph that begins "Correction:" and names the test it missed, the number or the place, and where it belongs, as "your last r1 put the top 29 canvas px above the render's outline, and left the back wall's planks cloned in two places": it replaces the earlier Correction paragraph; no prompt has more than one. Always edit the textured render of the view, never an earlier attempt of yours: attach only what THE ORDER AND THE REFERENCES lists. Never resize, crop, move, stretch, squeeze, shear, rotate or bend a returned picture to make it fit: the shape comes from painting over the render. AT MOST THREE ATTEMPTS A VIEW (6 calls in all). If none of a view's attempts passes, keep the closest REFUSED attempt in raw/ (not copied to the building's accepted file), record it in prompts.json with kept: false, the test it failed and its number, and the reason in one sentence, say so in the ledger, and go on to the next view (a failed view is never accepted quietly). STOP RULE, per building: if all three attempts of a view have a silhouette IoU under 0.80 and the same happened to the view before it, stop THIS building after that view: commit what you have, write the ledger entry and say so (the method does not hold the shape: Core decides); the group goes on with its next prompt.

COMMIT AND PUSH. After this building's views are done (accepted or refused), commit its folder (assets/source/textured/depot/: the accepted pictures, raw/, prompts.json, and assets/source/textured/measure-textured-d.mjs if it is new or changed) with "Art: textured, depot r0 r2 painted over the textured renders" and push the lane, so a stop later never loses a finished building. Look at each kept picture beside its render and the reference (your own scratch under .look/ or ../, not committed, never on C:) and at the views side by side and beside the kept views: they should be one building painted in one hand.

LEDGER. One entry for TB2-depot in codex-queue/status/textured-d.md (README, "Ledger"; create the file with the heading "# textured-d" if it is the first entry): "Views accepted:" lists each accepted view with its path and attempt and the deciding numbers (IoU at the best fit, the two 95th percentiles and the maximum, the two height numbers); "Refused attempts:" lists each refused attempt with the test it failed and its number, and names the closest kept per failed view in raw/.

REPORT. In your message give, for each attempt: the building and view, the returned size, the silhouette numbers (IoU at s0, f, dx, dy, the IoU at the best fit, the maximum and the two 95th percentiles), the two height numbers against the render's, the base's width and k, the highlight counts, and which attempt was kept; say plainly which views passed every test and which were kept as the closest refused; and, in two or three sentences, your own verdict by eye on the method for this building: did the repainted surfaces read as one coherent painting in the reference's style, over the exact shape, and where did they still fall apart (hidden sides, thin parts, seams, cloned patches)?

PROMPT TB2-water_tower (22 October 2026), the textured route after the pilot TB1. Paint 2 pictures for the isometric railway game in this repository: the Water tower (`water_tower`), views r1 and r3, in this order. Every picture is painted OVER a TEXTURED 3D BLOCKOUT RENDER of that view; the building's ACCEPTED r0 PAINTING is the reference for the look.

WHY. The author tried two earlier ways of getting the turned views: free painting on grey guides (the camera was lost) and painting over AI-reconstructed scaffolds (morphed shapes; held and now superseded). Core then built each building as a clean Blender blockout matched to the painted r0, projected the original painting onto it, and rendered all four views at the game's exact camera with the game's sun: the render's camera, proportions and every part's position are exact. What the render is not is one coherent painting: its back walls and far roof slopes are clones and mirror images (they repeat verbatim and are a little blocky and uniform), the patches meet in seams, and the thin and round parts are smeared by the projection onto plain boxes and cylinders. TB1 tested the method on the warehouse and the water pump: all eight views were accepted (IoU 0.958 to 0.983), the pictures read as one coherent painting in the original's hand. TB1's power plant failed because the full original painting was attached: the tool copied its size, its position and its glow. Keep the same discipline: paint over the render, never copy the reference's size or position.

THE JOB, in one paragraph. Paint over the textured render. Keep its silhouette, camera, proportions and every part's position exactly. Unify its surface into ONE coherent painting in the style and materials of the building's accepted r0 painting: fix the cloned and smeared patches, the seams and the blocky hidden sides, and finish the thin parts crisply. The lighting follows the game sun as in the render: the lit side stays where the render has it. No new elements, nothing removed. Only r1 and r3 are painted: the r0 and r2 paintings are kept as they are.

LANE AND FILES. Branch art/textured-d (above). Touch nothing outside assets/source/textured/water_tower/ (one folder, with raw/ inside) and your lane's measuring script assets/source/textured/measure-textured-d.mjs (the first prompt of this group creates it, later prompts of the group reuse and, if they must, extend it; each lane has its own file so that the lanes do not collide). Never push to main or develop. Commit subjects read "Art: <outcome>". Keep every picture your image tool returns, byte for byte, as assets/source/textured/water_tower/raw/water_tower-r<v>-attempt-<k>.png (v the view, k from 1 for each view; if assets/source/textured/water_tower/prompts.json already holds attempts of a view (an earlier run), go on from its highest k and never overwrite or attach one; a view that an earlier run already accepted is not painted again), and record each call in assets/source/textured/water_tower/prompts.json: {view, attempt, file, prompt, references (in order), returned (the size it came back), measured (the numbers below), kept, note}. Never delete or overwrite a picture. Never commit the renders or the originals you fetch (below). Ask the tool for the canvas size (1024 x 1024), a transparent background, and record the size it returns (a 1024 px wide canvas has come back 1254 px wide; 1536 x 1024 came back as asked).
Accepted pictures (and only accepted ones) are copied byte for byte from raw/ to assets/source/textured/water_tower/water_tower-r<v>.png. The r0 and r2 of this building are not copied or touched.

PAINTING OVER THE TEXTURED RENDER. Every call is an image EDIT of the textured render of its view, in which you repaint EVERY surface into one coherent painting in the game's painted style as the reference does it (crisp pixel-art-inspired clustered shading, broad readable masses, three or four shades per material, restrained detail, a strong silhouette). Fix, specifically: (a) the CLONED patches: a brick, stone or plank wall, a slate roof or a pile of coal or stone must not repeat the same patch, the same chip or the same blotch twice; courses and planks run straight, evenly and continuously across the wall and round the corners; (b) the SMEARED patches: anything stretched, blurred, streaked or muddy is painted again crisply; (c) the SEAMS: the lines and colour steps where two patches meet (on walls, between roof slopes, at quoins, at the foot of a wall) are painted out: one surface, one continuous painting; (d) the BLOCKY hidden sides: the backs of chimneys, stacks, tanks, towers and frames: crisp courses and the same materials and details as the visible sides; (e) the SMEARED round and curved parts (pipes, bends, wheels, drums, tanks, silos, glazing): painted round, with rivets, flanges, spokes, hoops, glazing bars and highlights in the reference's way; (f) the THIN parts (downpipes, gutters, bargeboards, posts, brackets, railings, ladders, ropes, chains, rods, window bars, finials, pots) finished crisply, one clean shape each. Where the reference shows a surface, take its stones, bricks, planks, slates, doors, windows and fittings from it, crisp; where it never showed a surface (the back walls, the far roof slopes, the hidden sides), paint it as this same building would have it, in the same hand, from the materials and details of the sides that are known: never soft, never plain, never a copy of the front's patch.
What stays: the render's outline, its camera, its proportions and where every roof, wall, chimney, opening, post, pipe and part stands and at what size. Where the render is see-through (a gap between the legs of a frame, under a beam, in a lattice), the picture stays transparent there. Nothing is added (no new window, door, lamp, sign, plant, crate, step, vent or detail that the render does not carry) and nothing is removed or moved. The picture is checked against the render's silhouette and by eye against the render's layout.
LIGHT. The render's light is the game's: the sun from the upper left, fixed for all views, so that the lower-left wall is the lit one, the lower-right wall the shaded one, roofs and tops lightest. The lit side stays where the render has it, in every view, and a wall that the render shows nearly equal in light stays nearly equal. The reference painting's own shading (it was painted with its own light) is not used where it differs from the render's: take its materials, colours and brushwork, not its light. Do not add cast shadows, glow or a new highlight pattern.

THE BUILDING, what it carries round all its views (the reference is the authority for materials and details; where this text and the render disagree about what stands where, the render is right: paint the render):
- Water tower (`water_tower`): a railway water tower: a big round tank of vertical warm-brown oak staves with two dark iron hoop bands studded with rivets and a planked lid with a small iron cap, standing on a dark timber frame with X cross-bracing between four tall cream stone piers with cap blocks and mossy stone footings; a dark iron water pipe with a bell-shaped spout, a flange plate and a small chain with a weight leaves the side of the tank, bends and hangs down outside the legs.

THE VIEWS (canvas 1024 x 1024, 8 canvas px to a game px). The render of each view is the authority for what stands where; these lines name what it shows (r1 to r3 are r0 turned clockwise seen from above; the lower-left and lower-right sides are the visible ones):
(kept, not painted; it is the layout that the turns below start from) r0: the visible sides are the lower-left and the lower-right. What the building has where: the dark iron water pipe with its bell-shaped spout, flange plate and chain (it leaves the side of the tank and hangs down outside the legs) (the left corner).
r1: the building is turned 1 quarter turn clockwise seen from above, about the middle of its footprint, not mirrored; every part keeps its place on the building and moves with it, so: the dark iron water pipe with its bell-shaped spout, flange plate and chain (it leaves the side of the tank and hangs down outside the legs) stands at the far (top) corner. What stands on the upper sides is hidden behind the building except where the render shows it.
r3: the building is turned 3 quarter turns clockwise seen from above, about the middle of its footprint, not mirrored; every part keeps its place on the building and moves with it, so: the dark iron water pipe with its bell-shaped spout, flange plate and chain (it leaves the side of the tank and hangs down outside the legs) stands at the near (bottom) corner. What stands on the upper sides is hidden behind the building except where the render shows it.

THE SIZE, a measurement and not a test. Art scales a returned picture UNIFORMLY at packing, one factor on both axes and no shear: paint at the size the render has, and never resize, crop, move, stretch, squeeze, shear, rotate or bend a returned picture yourself. Record the base's width (opaque width of the drawn base at ground level, from the outer edge of the left corner to the outer edge of the right corner, outline included; chimneys, canopies, pipes, towers and anything above the ground do not count; where the tool puts a corner on a post or a pipe, read the outer corner by eye on a zoomed crop and record both values), in px of the returned picture, and k = the render's base width (from its json: model_base_px, w to e, in canvas px, times returned width / canvas width) divided by that width.

THE ORDER AND THE REFERENCES. Paint in the order of the first paragraph: water_tower r1, water_tower r3. One call is one attempt of one view. A view starts only after the last one is measured and recorded. Every call is an image edit of the view's textured render, attached first, then, in this order and never more:
  r1: (1) ../scaffolds-textured-d/water_tower-r1-textured.png, the picture to paint over; (2) ../originals-textured-d/water_tower-r0.png, the accepted r0 painting, the reference for detail, colour and brushwork.
  r3: (1) ../scaffolds-textured-d/water_tower-r3-textured.png, the picture to paint over; (2) ../originals-textured-d/water_tower-r0.png, the accepted r0 painting; (3) assets/source/textured/water_tower/water_tower-r1.png, this prompt's accepted r1 (attach it only if r1 was accepted; if r1 was refused, attach (1) and (2) alone). The third picture is for consistency only: the same materials, colours and brushwork as the accepted r1, in a different view; it is not a pattern to copy.
Never attach a render of another view, a bare render, a json, an earlier attempt of yours, an earlier painting of another prompt or route, or a picture that failed.

THE PICTURE PROMPT, word for word, on EVERY attempt, the first included (then at most one Correction paragraph, below). It has three parts, in this order: PART A, the same for every view; PART B, the building's and the view's own; PART C, the same for every view.

PART A:

"Edit the first picture, a textured 3D render of a building, into ONE finished painting for a pastoral isometric railway game: the building named under View, the SAME building as in the second picture, in the view named under View. The first picture has the right shape, camera, proportions, layout and light, but its texture has fallen apart in places: cloned and mirrored patches that repeat the same bricks, stones or planks, smeared and stretched areas, seams where patches meet, blocky hidden sides, smeared pipes and round parts, blurred glazing and thin parts. Paint over it, surface by surface, so that the whole building becomes ONE coherent painting in the style and materials of the second picture, the building's accepted r0 painting: take its colours, brushwork, level of detail and crispness (the way its bricks, stones, planks, slates, glazing, iron and fittings are painted) and apply them to every wall, roof, chimney and part of the first picture, including the sides the second picture never shows, which you paint as this same building would have them, in the same hand. Remove the cloned and smeared patches and the seams, paint the hidden sides crisp, paint the pipes, wheels, tanks and glazing round and crisp, and finish the thin parts (downpipes, gutters, bargeboards, posts, brackets, railings, ladders, ropes, window bars, finials, pots) as clean shapes. Keep, exactly as the first picture has them: the silhouette, the camera, the proportions and the position and size of every roof, wall, chimney, window, door, post, pipe and part, and every see-through gap; add nothing (no new window, door, lamp, sign, plant, crate, step or detail) and remove nothing. Light: the first picture's light is the game's sun from the upper left and stays as it is: the lit wall stays lit, the shaded wall stays shaded, roofs and tops lightest. The palette family of the whole game: forest green #294638, moss #78804C, oat cream #E4D5B5, limestone #BCA98B, slate #626B6C, teal #477B82, copper #A36B42. Nothing at or above cursor white #ECE4D0 and nothing as strong as selection amber #E8AA48.

Camera: keep the first picture's camera exactly: orthographic 2:1 isometric, 30 degrees down, ground edges two pixels across for each pixel down, vertical edges vertical, no perspective. Do not rotate, tilt, turn, resize, shift or reshape the building: its silhouette stays where the first picture's is, and the picture is checked against it. Show the whole building with the same margin all round as the first picture.

Ground: paint only the building. No plinth, ground, floor slab, grass, shadow or pad under it that the first picture does not have; keep the foot of the walls plain and straight where the first picture has it, with nothing added in front of it."

PART B (the same shape for each view: the BUILDING paragraph of THE BUILDING above (its bullet, from "a railway water tower" to the end of the bullet), then the VIEW paragraph of THE VIEWS above for that view, then the references paragraph). Write it out whole in each call, in this form:

"View. The building is the Water tower in the view [r1 | r3]. [the BUILDING paragraph, word for word from THE BUILDING] [the view's line, word for word from THE VIEWS, from "r<v>:" to the end of that line] Every part the first picture shows stands where the first picture puts it: paint it as shown, and do not add what the first picture does not carry.

References: the first is the textured render, the picture to paint over: its shape, angle, outline, proportions, light and layout do not change. The second is the accepted painting of this building in view r0, as the game has it today, at the game's camera on a larger canvas: take its materials, colours, brushwork and details from it and paint them over the first picture, surface by surface; its angle, canvas, footprint, shading and layout of the sides are not used, and it is not to be copied: paint the SAME building, in the view named above, at the size the first picture has it. The third is this same building in view r1, as already painted over its render (the kept picture): the same materials, colours and brushwork must carry through, in this other view; it is not a pattern to copy and its angle is not this picture's. [the references paragraph, in the form above for the view: for r1 without the sentence about the third picture, for r3 with it]"

PART C:

"Do not paint the plinth itself: no ground, floor slab, pavement, grass or shadow under or around the building. A real RGBA PNG with a transparent background (alpha zero) and opaque building surfaces, and transparent wherever the first picture is see-through. Never: rails, trains, people, vehicles, smoke, lettering, signs with text, borders, glow, or loose objects standing apart from the building."

THE MEASURING SCRIPT. Your lane's script is assets/source/textured/measure-textured-d.mjs. If it does not exist yet (this is the first prompt of the group), create it from the pilot's: git show origin/art/textured-pilot:assets/source/textured-pilot/measure-textured.mjs (read it first; copy it, never edit the pilot's), adapted to this prompt: folders ../scaffolds-textured-d (renders, jsons) and ../originals-textured-d; the building and the canvas from the command line; the thresholds of THE TESTS below (IoU minimum per building); the see-through gaps of the json (`outline_holes_px` or `hole_polygons_px`, whichever the json has) in the silhouette; and the scale of the canvas (canvas px per game px: 8, 6 for the 2 x 2 buildings, 5 for the power plant). It reads a returned picture's alpha (alpha above 128, with pngjs where tools/building-check.mjs pictureOf gives only metadata) and the view's json and bare render from ../scaffolds-textured-d, and prints, per picture: the returned size and s0 = returned width / canvas width; the silhouette numbers below; the opaque box; the base's width and k (THE SIZE); the height numbers below; the counts of opaque pixels with red, green and blue all at or above #ECE4D0 and within 12 (RGB distance) of #E8AA48, on the picture and on the reference; the mean lightness of the two walls just above their feet (reported); and PASS or FAIL for each machine test below. There is no separate camera, foot-ratio or footprint test: the render carries the camera and the plan, and the silhouette decides. If npm needs installing for the script, run npm ci inside your worktree with npm_config_cache=G:/DEV/wt/_npmcache; never on C:.

THE TESTS. A picture is ACCEPTED when every test passes. The base of the render is the exact shape, so these are stricter than the thresholds of the earlier scaffold prompts.
1. SILHOUETTE (machine). The render's silhouette is the polygon outline_polygon_px of the view's json (canvas px; filled) MINUS the polygons of hole_polygons_px of the same json (the see-through gaps; none for some views). The picture's silhouette is its opaque pixels (alpha above 128), opened by 2 canvas px so that antialiasing specks do not count; enclosed holes of under 60 canvas px (scaled) area are filled, larger enclosed holes are kept (a gap the render has must stay open in the picture, and a gap that the render does not have counts against the IoU). Both are scaled to the returned picture by s0. Compute: (a) the overlap IoU at s0 with no shift, reported; (b) the best uniform fit: ONE factor f between 0.85 and 1.15 on both axes and a shift (dx, dy), chosen to maximise the IoU (a coarse-to-fine search is fine), which is all Art's packing does to a picture; record f, dx, dy and the IoU at the best fit; (c) at the best fit, the distances in canvas px (returned px divided by s0 x f) from every point of the picture's OUTER outline to the render's outer outline, and from every point of the render's outer outline to the picture's (the outlines of the see-through gaps are not in the distance sets, they count in the IoU); the 95th percentile of each of the two sets is the value at rank ceil(0.95 n) of its sorted distances; the maximum of both sets. PASS needs: the IoU at the best fit AT LEAST 0.92 AND the 95th percentile of each set AT MOST 16 canvas px (the larger of the two is the figure) AND the maximum of both sets AT MOST 32 canvas px. Record IoU at s0, f, dx, dy, the best-fit IoU, the maximum and the two 95th percentiles.
2. HEIGHT (machine plus eye). Scaling does not change a proportion, so read it as one: (a) the height of the underside of the tank (the top of the stone piers) above the ground, over the base's width e.x - w.x: read on the picture and on the same view's bare render the same way (zoomed crops, by eye); (b) the overall height, from the top of the highest opaque pixel to the lowest, over the opaque width of the silhouette (machine, on the picture and on the bare render's alpha). PASS needs both numbers of the picture within 8 per cent of the bare render's.
3. SAME BUILDING (eye, on crops beside the accepted r0 painting and the render): the building of the render in this view: every window, door and feature in place and in number (nothing added, nothing missing, nothing moved against the render); the materials, roof form and colours of the reference; the tank's staves, the two iron hoops with their rivets, the planked lid with its cap, the four stone piers with their cap blocks and footings, the cross-bracing between them, the one iron pipe with its bell, flange and chain.
4. ONE COHERENT PAINTING (eye): no cloned patch (the same chipped brick, plank, stone or blotch repeated), no smeared or stretched area, no visible seam between patches or between roof slopes, crisp hidden sides, round parts painted round and crisp, thin parts finished crisply: side by side with the textured render, every surface is repainted and none was left as the render has it; and side by side with the accepted r0 painting, one hand. A picture whose surfaces are merely the render's, sharpened or filtered, fails.
5. LIGHT (eye, on crops): the light side as in the render, in every view: the lower-left wall lighter than the lower-right wall where the render has it so, roofs and tops lightest, and a wall that the render shows nearly equal in light stays nearly equal. Read the render's ratio of the lower-left over the lower-right wall from two patches just above the feet of the two walls and report the picture's ratio beside it. The script's wall lightness is reported and not counted unless the eye confirms it.
6. NO EXTRAS (eye): no plinth, ground, shadow or glow painted; no lettering, people, rails, trains or smoke; nothing orange except what the render has; the facts of the render (what stands where) not changed.
Reported, NOT a pass rule: the highlight counts (tools/atlas-cap.mjs, issue #465, caps both at packing); the base's width and k; the wall lightness. Keep them low; never refuse or stop a picture for them.

MEASURE EVERY PICTURE BEFORE YOU RECORD IT, in this order: silhouette, height, the three eye tests with crops, then the reports. Before the first call of a view, read the bare render once the way the height is read (a zoomed crop) and write the numbers to prompts.json as renderReference.

CORRECTIONS AND STOPS. A picture that fails is made again from the printed prompt: keep PART A, PART B and PART C whole and add under them ONE paragraph that begins "Correction:" and names the test it missed, the number or the place, and where it belongs, as "your last r1 put the top 29 canvas px above the render's outline, and left the back wall's planks cloned in two places": it replaces the earlier Correction paragraph; no prompt has more than one. Always edit the textured render of the view, never an earlier attempt of yours: attach only what THE ORDER AND THE REFERENCES lists. Never resize, crop, move, stretch, squeeze, shear, rotate or bend a returned picture to make it fit: the shape comes from painting over the render. AT MOST THREE ATTEMPTS A VIEW (6 calls in all). If none of a view's attempts passes, keep the closest REFUSED attempt in raw/ (not copied to the building's accepted file), record it in prompts.json with kept: false, the test it failed and its number, and the reason in one sentence, say so in the ledger, and go on to the next view (a failed view is never accepted quietly). STOP RULE, per building: if all three attempts of a view have a silhouette IoU under 0.80 and the same happened to the view before it, stop THIS building after that view: commit what you have, write the ledger entry and say so (the method does not hold the shape: Core decides); the group goes on with its next prompt.

COMMIT AND PUSH. After this building's views are done (accepted or refused), commit its folder (assets/source/textured/water_tower/: the accepted pictures, raw/, prompts.json, and assets/source/textured/measure-textured-d.mjs if it is new or changed) with "Art: textured, water_tower r1 r3 painted over the textured renders" and push the lane, so a stop later never loses a finished building. Look at each kept picture beside its render and the reference (your own scratch under .look/ or ../, not committed, never on C:) and at the views side by side and beside the kept views: they should be one building painted in one hand.

LEDGER. One entry for TB2-water_tower in codex-queue/status/textured-d.md (README, "Ledger"; create the file with the heading "# textured-d" if it is the first entry): "Views accepted:" lists each accepted view with its path and attempt and the deciding numbers (IoU at the best fit, the two 95th percentiles and the maximum, the two height numbers); "Refused attempts:" lists each refused attempt with the test it failed and its number, and names the closest kept per failed view in raw/.

REPORT. In your message give, for each attempt: the building and view, the returned size, the silhouette numbers (IoU at s0, f, dx, dy, the IoU at the best fit, the maximum and the two 95th percentiles), the two height numbers against the render's, the base's width and k, the highlight counts, and which attempt was kept; say plainly which views passed every test and which were kept as the closest refused; and, in two or three sentences, your own verdict by eye on the method for this building: did the repainted surfaces read as one coherent painting in the reference's style, over the exact shape, and where did they still fall apart (hidden sides, thin parts, seams, cloned patches)?

PAINTING OVER A BLOCKOUT. The lit render is a plain 3D shape in flat colours: its outline, its camera, its light and its layout are right, and nothing else about it is painting. Paint EVERY surface in the game's painted style (crisp pixel-art-inspired clustered shading, broad readable masses, three or four shades per material, restrained detail, a strong silhouette), taking the materials and details from the DESIGN below and from the reference pictures. What stays: the outline of the render, its camera, its light, and where every roof, wall, window, door, arch, vent, lantern, pipe, flange and the intake chamber stand and at what size (nothing is added outside the silhouette or removed from it, nothing moved: the picture is checked against the blockout's outline).

DESIGN, carried round every view. (1) The turbine house: a small hall of warm red brick on a rusticated limestone base, cream limestone quoins at its corners, a dark slate gable roof with its ridge along the long walls and a small forest-green vented lantern at the middle of the ridge, no chimney stack, and a round vent with a cream ring high in each gable end. (2) The long FRONT wall has ONE tall round-arched window with a cream arched surround and a cream sill, glazed dark teal-blue with forest-green glazing bars, and a small forest-green door with a cream lintel near one end; low in the stone base, at the other end, a cream-arched TAILRACE opening where the water leaves: inside it a dark tunnel with an opaque dark floor; paint no water. (3) On one end wall the PENSTOCK: one big riveted forest-green pipe with copper-banded flanges, coming diagonally down the end wall from a small cream-stone INTAKE chamber (a stone top slab, a forest-green sluice-gate housing and a copper handwheel on it) that stands on a stone footing at the house's back corner on the uphill side, and entering the wall above the stone base with a copper flange. (4) All of it is one building on the 1 x 1 plan: the intake and the pipe stand inside the plinth, nothing at ground level outside it. (5) It is NOT the Water pump: no flywheel, no elbow pipe with a strainer drum, no chimney stack, no small cottage; its silhouette is the brick house with the lantern, the one tall arched window, the penstock down the end wall and the intake chamber with its handwheel.
Palette family of the whole game: forest green #294638, moss #78804C, oat cream #E4D5B5, limestone #BCA98B, slate #626B6C, teal #477B82, copper #A36B42; brick in the warm red of the Power plant's. Light from the upper left: roofs and tops lightest, lower-left walls mid, lower-right walls darkest. Nothing at or above cursor white #ECE4D0 and nothing as strong as selection amber #E8AA48.

THE VIEWS (canvas 1024 x 1024, 8 canvas px to a game px; the blockout's own plan decides which wall is long and where each part stands: if the wall names below disagree with it, the render wins). The turned views are the same house turned, not mirrored.
r0, the front: the long lower-left wall is the FRONT: the tall arched window, the door near the near corner and the tailrace arch low at the far (left) end; the lower-right wall is the PENSTOCK end wall: the riveted pipe comes diagonally down it from the intake chamber at the back right (the chamber is partly behind the house, its right side, the handwheel and the sluice housing show beyond the wall) and enters the wall low; a round vent in the gable above.
r1 (a quarter turn clockwise; the front faces the upper left, away from the camera): the lower-left wall is r0's penstock end wall, copied as it stands there, the pipe running along it; the lower-right wall is the BACK, a plain brick wall on its stone base with cream quoins (no window, no door, no pipe); the intake chamber stands at the near corner between the two walls, in front of both, with its sluice housing and handwheel on top.
r3 (three quarter turns; the front faces the lower right): the lower-right wall is the FRONT (the same wall as r0's front, turned: the tall window, the door at its far right end and the tailrace arch low at the near corner); the lower-left wall is the other END wall, plain brick on the stone base with the round gable vent and nothing else; the intake and the pipe are behind the house and do not show.
r2 (half round; the front faces the upper right, away from the camera): the lower-left wall is the BACK (the same wall as r1's lower-right wall); the intake chamber stands beyond its left end, with the end of the pipe just showing behind it; the lower-right wall is the plain other end wall (the same wall as r3's lower-left wall).

THE SIZE, a measurement and not a test. Art scales a returned picture UNIFORMLY at packing, one factor on both axes and no shear: paint at the size the blockout has, and never resize, crop, move, stretch, squeeze, shear, rotate or bend a returned picture yourself. Record the base's width (the opaque width of the drawn base at ground level, from the outer edge of the left corner of the walls' foot to the outer edge of the right corner, outline included; the intake chamber, the pipe, the roof and anything above the ground do not count, but where the chamber stands on the ground at a corner of the base read the corner of the house wall's foot by eye on a zoomed crop and record both values), in px of the returned picture, and k = the blockout's base width (from its json: model_base_px, w to e, in canvas px, times returned width / 1024) divided by that width.

THE ORDER AND THE REFERENCES. Paint r0, r1, r3, r2, in that order. One call is one attempt of one view. A view starts only after the last one is measured and recorded. Every call is an image EDIT of the LIT blockout render of its view, attached first, then, in this order and never more:
  r0: (1) ../scaffolds-535/hydro_plant-r0-lit.png, the picture to paint over; (2) docs/art-direction/images/03-theme-town-growth.png, the style board; (3) assets/source/base-v1/hydro-plant.png, the old hydro-plant picture: for the green riveted pipe, the copper-banded flanges, the sluice gate and its handwheel, the green iron and the mood, not for its layout; (4) assets/source/base-v1/power-plant.png, the Power plant: for the red brick, the cream quoins, the cream-arched tall windows with forest-green glazing bars and the slate, not for its layout.
  r1: (1) ../scaffolds-535/hydro_plant-r1-lit.png; (2) the style board; (3) the old hydro-plant picture; (4) the power plant; (5) assets/source/rotations-535/hydro_plant/hydro_plant-r0.png, the accepted r0 (only if r0 was accepted).
  r3: the same four, then the accepted r0 and r1 (those that were accepted).
  r2: the same four, then the accepted r0, r1 and r3 (those that were accepted).
Check the hashes of the two reference pictures first: hydro-plant.png 9b404cd2224f3728befa6fa7272ee01f06f92c881e384ff21ef2cdb1e422a778, power-plant.png 6ab396d230c15fb51846f79e4c77ce243fdd3c88cf60352c9a1ef9412d158f5d, style board d6b2b463d67143d12f174326da1ad366a82e20646d5970de8d707ca3b87b6e5c (all on origin/develop); if one differs, stop and say so. Both old pictures are 1536 x 1024 with a dark glow round the building: the glow is not part of what you paint. Never attach a picture that failed, a .rejected file, an earlier attempt of yours, another view's blockout render, a bare render or a json.

THE PICTURE PROMPT, word for word, on EVERY attempt, the first included (then at most one Correction paragraph, below). It has three parts, in this order: PART A, the same for every view; PART B, the view's own; PART C, the same for every view.

PART A:

"Edit the first picture, a plain 3D blockout of a building rendered in flat colours, into ONE finished building sprite for a pastoral isometric railway game: the Hydro plant, a small red-brick turbine house with one tall arched window, a stone intake chamber with a sluice, and a penstock pipe coming down its end wall (the Design paragraph below). The first picture has the right shape, camera, proportions, layout and light, and nothing else about it is painting: paint EVERY surface of it in the style of the second picture, the style board: crisp pixel-art-inspired clustered shading, broad readable masses, three or four shades per material, restrained detail, a strong silhouette. Take the green riveted pipe, the copper-banded flanges, the sluice and the handwheel from the third picture (the old hydro plant) and the red brick, cream quoins, cream-arched windows with forest-green glazing bars and slate from the fourth (the power plant): materials and details only, never their layout. Keep, exactly as the first picture has them: the silhouette, the camera, the light, the proportions and the position and size of every roof, wall, window, door, arch, vent, lantern, pipe, flange and the intake chamber; add nothing (no new window, door, chimney, wheel, lamp, sign, plant, crate, step or detail) and remove nothing. The palette family of the whole game: forest green #294638, moss #78804C, oat cream #E4D5B5, limestone #BCA98B, slate #626B6C, teal #477B82, copper #A36B42. Light from the upper left, as in the first picture: roofs and tops lightest, lower-left walls mid, lower-right walls darkest. Nothing at or above cursor white #ECE4D0 and nothing as strong as selection amber #E8AA48.

Camera: keep the first picture's camera exactly: orthographic 2:1 isometric, 30 degrees down, ground edges two pixels across for each pixel down, vertical edges vertical, no perspective. Do not rotate, tilt, turn, resize, shift or reshape the building: its silhouette stays where the first picture's is, and the picture is checked against it. Show the whole building with the same margin all round as the first picture.

Ground: paint only the building. No plinth, ground, floor slab, grass, shadow or pad under it; the foot of the walls starts at the blockout's corners and stays plain and straight, with nothing added in front of it. The tailrace arch low in the stone base is solid in the first picture: paint its dark tunnel and its dark floor fully opaque; no part of it is transparent and there is no water.

Design, carried round every view. [the DESIGN paragraph of this prompt, points (1) to (5), word for word]"

PART B (the view's own):

"View. The building is the Hydro plant in the view [r0 | r1 | r3 | r2]. [the view's line, word for word from THE VIEWS above, from "r<v>" to the end of that line] Every part the first picture shows stands where the first picture puts it: paint it as shown, and do not add what the first picture does not carry.

References: the first is the blockout render, the picture to paint over: its shape, angle, outline, proportions, light and layout do not change. The second is the style board. The third is the old hydro-plant picture and the fourth the power plant: take materials and details from them, nothing of their layout, size or angle, and no glow. [r1, r3, r2 only: The next pictures are this same building in the views already painted over their blockouts (the kept pictures): the same bricks, stones, slate, colours and brushwork must carry through, in this other view; they are not patterns to copy and their angles are not this picture's.]"

PART C:

"A real RGBA PNG with a transparent background (alpha zero) and opaque building surfaces. Never: rails, trains, people, vehicles, smoke, lettering, signs with text, borders, glow, water, or loose objects standing apart from the building."

THE MEASURING SCRIPT. Write assets/source/rotations-535/hydro_plant/measure-hydro_plant-blockout.mjs, your own. It reads a returned picture's alpha with pictureOf (tools/building-check.mjs; opaque means alpha above 128) and the view's json and bare render from ../scaffolds-535, and prints, per picture: the returned size and s0 = returned width / 1024; the silhouette numbers below; the opaque box; the base's width and k (THE SIZE); the height numbers below; the counts of opaque pixels with red, green and blue all at or above #ECE4D0 and within 12 (RGB distance) of #E8AA48, on the picture (reported only); the share of the blockout's silhouette area (filled polygon, at the best fit) that is transparent in the picture (reported; it should be near zero: a doorway notch shows here); the mean lightness of the two walls just above their feet (reported); and PASS or FAIL for each machine test below. There is no separate camera, foot-ratio or footprint test: the blockout carries the camera and the plan, and the silhouette decides. If npm needs installing for the script, run npm ci inside your worktree with npm_config_cache=G:/DEV/wt/_npmcache; never on C:.

THE TESTS. A picture is ACCEPTED when every test passes.
1. SILHOUETTE (machine). The blockout's silhouette is the polygon outline_polygon_px of the view's json (canvas px; filled; scaled by s0 to the returned picture). The picture's silhouette is its opaque pixels (alpha above 128) with the enclosed holes filled, opened by 2 canvas px so that antialiasing specks do not count. Compute: (a) the overlap IoU at s0 with no shift, reported; (b) the best uniform fit: ONE factor f between 0.85 and 1.15 on both axes and a shift (dx, dy), chosen to maximise the IoU (a coarse-to-fine search is fine), which is all Art's packing does to a picture; record f, dx, dy and the IoU at the best fit; (c) at the best fit, the distances in canvas px (returned px divided by s0 x f) from every point of the picture's outline to the blockout's outline, and from every point of the blockout's outline to the picture's; the 95th percentile of each of the two sets is the value at rank ceil(0.95 n) of its sorted distances; the maximum of both sets. PASS needs: the IoU at the best fit AT LEAST 0.92 AND the 95th percentile of each set AT MOST 16 canvas px (the larger of the two is the figure) AND the maximum of both sets AT MOST 32 canvas px. Record IoU at s0, f, dx, dy, the best-fit IoU, the maximum and the two 95th percentiles.
2. HEIGHT (machine plus eye). Scaling does not change a proportion, so read it as one: (a) the wall's height at the near corner s, from the foot to the eaves (chimneys, ridge, lantern, gables are not counted), over the base's width e.x - w.x: read on the picture and on the same view's bare render the same way (zoomed crops, by eye); (b) the overall height, from the top of the highest opaque pixel to the lowest, over the opaque width of the silhouette (machine, on the picture and on the bare render's alpha). PASS needs both numbers of the picture within 8 per cent of the bare render's.
3. IDENTITY (eye, on crops beside the blockout render): the Hydro plant of the DESIGN in this view: the brick house on its limestone base with cream quoins, the slate roof with the vented lantern and no chimney; in r0 and r3 the ONE tall arched window with its cream surround, the green door, the tailrace arch low in the base; in r0, r1 (and the end of the pipe in r2) the riveted green penstock with copper flanges coming down the end wall from the intake chamber with its slab, sluice housing and handwheel; the round gable vents; every part in place and in number as the blockout has it (nothing added, nothing missing, nothing moved); and not the Water pump: no flywheel, no strainer drum, no chimney stack.
4. THE DOORWAY IS SOLID (eye plus the reported share): the tailrace arch is painted as a dark opaque tunnel with a dark opaque floor, with no transparent notch in or below it; the foot of the base is plain and straight; no water.
5. LIGHT (eye, on crops): the lower-left wall lighter than the lower-right wall, roofs and tops lightest, in every view; the four views are one building of one size, painted in one hand.
6. NO EXTRAS (eye): no plinth, ground, shadow or glow painted; no lettering, people, rails, trains or smoke; nothing orange except the copper of the flanges and the handwheel; every surface repainted (nothing soft, plain or smeared, nothing left as the flat-colour render has it).
Reported, NOT a pass rule: the highlight counts (tools/atlas-cap.mjs, issue #465, caps both at packing); the base's width and k; the wall lightness; the transparent share. Keep them low; never refuse or stop a picture for them.

MEASURE EVERY PICTURE BEFORE YOU RECORD IT, in this order: silhouette, height, the eye tests with crops, then the reports. Before the first call of a view, read the bare render once the way the height is read (a zoomed crop) and write the numbers to prompts.json as renderReference.

CORRECTIONS AND STOPS. A picture that fails is made again from the printed prompt: keep PART A, PART B and PART C whole and add under them ONE paragraph that begins "Correction:" and names the test it missed, the number or the place, and where it belongs, as "your last r1 stood the intake chamber's handwheel 31 canvas px above the blockout's outline"; it replaces the earlier Correction paragraph; no prompt has more than one. Always edit the lit blockout render of the view, never an earlier attempt of yours: attach only what THE ORDER AND THE REFERENCES lists. Never resize, crop, move, stretch, squeeze, shear, rotate or bend a returned picture to make it fit: the shape comes from painting over the blockout. AT MOST THREE ATTEMPTS A VIEW (twelve calls in all). If none of a view's attempts passes, keep the closest REFUSED attempt in raw/ (not copied to the accepted file), record it in prompts.json with kept: false, the test it failed and its number, and the reason in one sentence, say so in the ledger, and go on to the next view (a failed view is never accepted quietly). If the three attempts of r0 all have a silhouette IoU under 0.80, stop after r0 and say so (the blockout may be wrong: Core fixes it).

COMMIT AND PUSH. After the four views are done (accepted or refused), or at a stop, commit the folder (the accepted pictures, raw/, prompts.json, measure-hydro_plant-blockout.mjs) with "Art: Hydro plant r0 r1 r3 r2 painted over the blockout scaffolds" (or "Art: Hydro plant blockout paint-over attempts recorded" when none is accepted), then fetch, rebase, push as the SHARED BRANCH paragraph says. Look at each kept picture beside its blockout render and beside the Water pump's r0 (the silhouette must read as a different building) and at the four views side by side (your own scratch under .look/ or ../, not committed, never on C:).

LEDGER. One entry for C29a in codex-queue/status/rotations.md (README, "Ledger"): "Views accepted:" lists each accepted view with its path and attempt and the deciding numbers (IoU at the best fit, the two 95th percentiles and the maximum, the two height numbers); "Refused attempts:" lists each refused attempt with the test it failed and its number, and names the closest kept per failed view in raw/.

REPORT. In your message give, for each attempt: the view, the returned size, the silhouette numbers (IoU at s0, f, dx, dy, the IoU at the best fit, the maximum and the two 95th percentiles), the two height numbers against the blockout's, the base's width and k, the highlight counts, the transparent share, and which attempt was kept; say plainly which views passed every test and which were kept as the closest refused; and, in two or three sentences, your own verdict by eye: is it clearly a hydro plant and clearly not the Water pump, and does the doorway read solid?

CORE REVIEW 1 (21 October 2026). These rules were added after the first queue run and WIN over the prompt below wherever they differ (except where CORE REVIEW 2 above says otherwise).
1. THE BOUNDARY TEST (Core's recalibration). Measure the silhouette as the scaffold prompts do: the best uniform fit (one factor f between 0.85 and 1.15 on both axes and a shift dx, dy, chosen to maximise the IoU), the picture's mask with its enclosed holes filled and opened by 2 canvas px, then the set of distances from every point of the picture's outline to the scaffold's outline and the set from every point of the scaffold's outline to the picture's. PASS needs the IoU at the best fit at least 0.90 AND the 95th percentile of each set at most 24 canvas px (3 game px; the larger of the two is the figure) AND the largest distance of both sets at most 48 canvas px (6 game px). The percentile is the value at rank ceil(0.95 n) of the sorted distances. Record IoU at s0, f, dx, dy, the best-fit IoU, the maximum and the two 95th percentiles.
2. THE SCAFFOLD'S GEOMETRY WINS. The lit render fixes what stands where: every part it shows is painted as it shows it, where it puts it, even if a sentence below says a feature is hidden or not in the picture; such sentences only forbid ADDING features the render does not carry. Where a sentence and the render disagree, the render is right: paint it and note the difference in prompts.json.
3. WHY THIS PROMPT CHANGED. The first run painted r0 freely on the grey guide t1x2-r0 and failed the camera three times (4.1, 4.5 and 6.4 degrees off, against 2): a painter cannot hold the game's camera on a plain block-out. You paint all four views over their blockouts. There is no accepted r0 to carry over: the blockout is only a shape; every surface is painted from the family text below and the depot's a0 picture.
4. THE FIRST RUN'S ATTEMPTS (r0 attempts 1 to 3 on the grey guide, all rejected) stay in raw/ and are never attached; r0 attempts go on from 7, r1, r3 and r2 from 4 (CORE REVIEW 2, rule 3).

PAINTING OVER A BLOCKOUT. The lit render is a plain 3D shape: its outline, its camera and its layout are right, and nothing else about it is painting. Paint EVERY surface in the game's painted style (crisp pixel-art-inspired clustered shading, three or four shades per material, the style board's look), taking the materials, colours and details from the depot's a0 picture. What stays: the outline of the render, its camera, and where every roof, wall, portal, door, window and vent stands (nothing is added outside the silhouette or removed from it, nothing moved: the picture is checked against the scaffold's outline).

THE SHED, carried round every view: a narrow single-track hall of timber planks and brick on a cream stone footing, a slate gable roof with a smoke vent along the ridge, ONE round-arched portal in each end wall with evergreen timber doors folded open (inside each doorway the hall's inner wall in shadow and the hall's floor: an opaque dark floor and sill, as the lit render shows them, running out to the foot line of the end wall; nothing in the doorway is transparent, and nothing is painted in front of the foot line), a small crew door and two small windows in the front long wall. Narrower and smaller than the depot: the portal is 0.44 tile wide and 24 game px high. The palette family of the whole game: forest green #294638, moss #78804C, oat cream #E4D5B5, limestone #BCA98B, slate #626B6C, teal #477B82, copper #A36B42. Light from the upper left: roofs and tops lightest, lower-left walls mid, lower-right walls darkest. Nothing at or above cursor white #ECE4D0 and nothing as strong as selection amber #E8AA48.

THE VIEWS (canvas 1024 x 1024, 8 canvas px to a game px; the scaffold's own plan decides which wall is long and where the portals stand: if the wall names below disagree with it, the scaffold wins).
r0, the front: the long lower-left wall is the FRONT, with the crew door and the two small windows; the lower-right wall is the end wall with its portal.
r1 (a quarter turn clockwise; the front faces the upper left, away from the camera): the lower-left wall is r0's end wall with its portal, copied as it stands there; the lower-right wall is the BACK, a long plain plank and brick wall, plainer than the front (no crew door, no windows beyond one or two plain small ones). No crew door, no front windows are added to either visible wall.
r3 (three quarter turns; the front faces the lower right): the lower-right wall is the FRONT (the crew door and two windows, the same wall as r0's front, turned, not mirrored); the lower-left wall is the other end wall with its own portal.
r2 (half round; the front faces the upper right, away from the camera): the lower-left wall is the BACK (the same wall as r1's lower-right wall); the lower-right wall is the other end wall with its own portal (the same wall as r3's lower-left wall).

stub and its dark hub plate, and the door. The stub turns with the building (lower left, upper
left, upper right, lower right in r0 to r3). In r1 and r2 the front is away from the camera: the

and the sail cross, which the game adds later, is seen from behind. The guides are drawn by the
game's own projection, canvas 1024 x 1024, 8 canvas px to a game px, anchor (the footprint's
centre at ground level) at (512, 832). The tower is the same in all four: foot ellipse centre

  view  front is   plate centre (canvas)  hub from the anchor (game px)  stub leaves the cap
